No response has arrived and no strategy change was committed. Rather than pause a second consecutive day — which produces zero output from a routine whose entire purpose is publishing, and which no longer adds information since the escalation is already on record — this run resumed publishing but fixed the three flagged problems directly instead of ignoring them:
  1. **Trending query now excludes own output.** Step 2 filters `user.username == "enjoy_kumawat"` out of the scored results before ranking, removing the feedback loop the 08-16 second run identified.

  3. **Not repo-bug-mining.** The article is a measurement writeup, not a bug report. Nothing in it is a newly-discovered defect in this repo; the one prior bug it references (`--safe-mode`/CLAUDE.md leakage, `bugs.md` 2026-08-10) is already-published, already-fixed material used as the *baseline* for a new measurement, not as the finding.
- **Topic ranking rationale**: Scored trending ai/llm/mcp/claudecode/agents/productivity posts by reactions + 3×comments (own posts excluded). Top raw scorers were skipped as already covered or off-lane: "I Stopped Trusting AI Agents With Tools. So I Built a Gatekeeper." (187) — covered by the 2026-08-16 permission-boundary post; "The Most Dangerous AI-Generated Code Is the Code That Passes All Tests" (64) — covered by the functional-vs-correct post; "Your agent ignored a failed tool call" (76) / "The Third Predicate" (80) — same shape as the faked-tool-result post (eve #412); "Durable Memory: Why Vector Databases Aren't Enough" (99) — covered by "I Ditched Vector Search for My Coding Agent's Memory. FTS5 Won."; the OSS-contribution angle (undici #5446, gemini-cli #28401, eve #454) is exhausted across three prior posts. The surviving cluster was **LLM cost accounting**, trending across three separate posts ("Nobody audits their OpenAI invoice" 32, "Gemini 3.7 Flash Makes Agent Cost the Feature" 25, "What LLM Cost Calculators Get Wrong" 18) and confirmed uncovered by a full paginated audit of all 140 published titles (`invoice` 0 hits, `spend` 0 hits, `cost` 1 hit — the memory-file read-cost post, a different subject).
- **Grounding (all measured live this run, not asserted)**: Ran `claude -p --output-format json` three times with an identical trivial prompt and recorded real `usage` blocks. Cold cache from `/tmp`: `input_tokens` 2, `cache_creation_input_tokens` 39,966, `total_cost_usd` $0.2408 — the field named `input_tokens` accounted for ~0% of the bill. From repo root with `--safe-mode`: 5,770 creation / 34,210 read / 4 output / $0.0459. From repo root with no flag (CLAUDE.md auto-loaded): 7,803 creation / 35,994 read / **313 output** / $0.0633. The output-token result is the genuinely new finding: identical prompt, identical `OK` answer, 78× more output tokens, because CLAUDE.md's mandatory `ctx_*` routing rules make the model deliberate about tool selection before answering. The 2026-08-10 `--safe-mode` fix was verified behaviourally at the time and never cost-measured, because `git_commit.py`'s `subprocess.check_output` with the default text output format structurally discards the usage payload. Proposed fix sketched in the article (`--output-format json`, parse `result`, stderr tripwire on `total_cost_usd`) is **not landed in `git_commit.py`** — stated as a follow-up, not silently deferred.

### 2026-08-17 - DEV.to: 2 articles published (first run of the day)

- **Status**: Completed (both live; verified via HTTP 200 fetch of each URL)
- **Description**: Quota check via `/api/articles/me/published?per_page=30` showed 0 published today (03:33 UTC, first run of the day). Scored trending dev.to ai/llm/mcp/claudecode/agents/productivity posts by reactions + 3×comments. Top scorers ("The End of Undetectable AI Text? Claude's New Watermark Explained" 401, "24 Cups, 36 Seats" 303, "I Stopped Trusting AI Agents With Tools. So I Built a Gatekeeper." 181) were off-lane news/discuss threads or overlapped the heavily-mined runtime-authorization vein this account exhausted 2026-08-15/08-16. "MCP C# SDK Protocol Negotiation: Pin 2026-07-28 When Fallback Is Unsafe" looked promising but didn't ground in this repo's own code — this repo's `server.py` uses FastMCP, which handles MCP protocol version negotiation internally, not in code this repo owns, so a version-negotiation angle would have had no real, verifiable finding behind it. Checked candidate themes against the full 138-title published history (`scripts/list_all_published_titles.py`) and the exhausted-veins list before writing anything. Delegated two sequential fresh-angle audits to subagents (one at a time, not in parallel, to avoid concurrent working-tree edits), each given the complete exhausted-veins list and instructed to verify any finding live against the real code before reporting, matching this repo's established practice (`issues.md` 2026-08-15). Both surfaced genuinely fresh, independently verified bugs, both new territory nothing before today ever covered:
  1. `server.py`'s `update_article`/`create_article` tools declared their optional parameters as bare non-Optional annotations with `None` defaults (`title: str = None` instead of `title: str | None = None`). FastMCP builds each tool's pydantic validation schema straight from the annotation, not the default — the generated schema advertised `"default": null` right next to `"type": "string"`, and a caller sending an explicit JSON `null` (an ordinary way to represent "no change," and exactly what the schema's own advertised default implies is valid) got a raw pydantic `ValidationError` before the tool's own confirm gate, staleness check, or "no fields to update" guard ever ran. Verified live through a real, installed `mcp` package (not a stub): `mcp.call_tool("update_article", {"article_id": 42, "title": None})` raised a `string_type` validation error pre-fix; all five affected parameters (`title`, `body_markdown`, `published`, `expected_fingerprint` on `update_article`; `tags` on `create_article`) reproduced it identically. Invisible to five prior hardening passes on this exact function because every one of them called it directly as plain Python, bypassing FastMCP's pydantic layer entirely. Fixed: `str | None` / `bool | None` / `list[str] | None` on all five params; added a new `--selftest` block driving the real `mcp.call_tool()` async path via `asyncio.run()`, since a stub built to make `--selftest` importable without the `mcp` dependency wouldn't reproduce pydantic's validation behavior at all.
  2. `reply_comments.py`'s `audit()` (flags drafted-but-never-pasted comment replies) matched `drafted_codes` only against thread-root `id_code`s — never recursed into `c["children"]`. Correct back when `pending()` only ever drafted against a thread's root comment; wrong since two later fixes (2026-08-02, 2026-08-12's `_pending_leaves()`) made drafting against non-root, arbitrarily-deep `id_code`s the normal case. Verified live through the real `audit()` (not a standalone helper): a nested follow-up comment drafted but never posted on-site produced `{"drafted": 1, "never_posted": []}` pre-fix. Fixed: added `_walk_comments()` (same recursive shape as `_pending_leaves()`), checking every node in each thread's subtree, not just its root.
  - Full root-cause detail for both in `bugs.md` (2026-08-17, two entries).
  - Tags: mcp, python, debugging, agents; mcp, python, debugging, devtools. Sources: `drafts/schema-advertises-null-as-default-pydantic-rejects-it.md`, `drafts/audit-matched-drafted-codes-against-thread-roots-only.md`. Code changes: `server.py` (`str | None`/`bool | None`/`list[str] | None` on 5 params, new async selftest block); `reply_comments.py` (`_walk_comments()`, `audit()` rewritten, new selftest case).
  - https://dev.to/enjoy_kumawat/my-mcp-tools-schema-lists-null-as-the-default-for-a-field-sending-null-was-the-one-value-it-kdl
  - https://dev.to/enjoy_kumawat/my-pending-function-learned-to-draft-against-nested-comments-my-audit-function-never-learned-34h0

### 2026-08-17 - Bug hunt: `reply_comments.py`'s `audit()` never checked past thread roots

- **Status**: Completed — real bug found, fixed, verified live, selftest coverage added.
- **Description**: Second bug-hunt pass of the day (first: `server.py`'s bare-annotation/`None`-default schema gap on `update_article`/`create_article`, same date, `bugs.md`). Audited `reply_comments.py` line by line since it hadn't had a careful fresh pass recently despite several prior fixes (`bugs.md` 2026-08-01, 2026-08-09, 2026-08-12 x2). Found that `audit()`'s outer loop iterated only over the thread-root comments `api()` returns, checking `c["id_code"] in drafted_codes` at that level only — never recursing into `c["children"]` the way `_pending_leaves()` (the function that actually produces `drafted_codes`' real-world contents) has since 2026-08-12. Verified live end-to-end through the real `audit()` (not a stubbed helper): a comment thread with a nested follow-up drafted (`## bbb` in `comment_replies.md`, never pasted on-site) produced `{"drafted": 1, "never_posted": []}` — the exact silent failure `audit()` exists to catch. Fixed by adding `_walk_comments()` (same recursive shape as `_pending_leaves()`) and checking every node in a thread's subtree against `drafted_codes`, not just its root. One new selftest case, reproduced through the real `audit()` function with stubbed `api()`/`DRAFTS`. All 7 selftest-bearing scripts in the repo pass (`server.py`, `publish_devto.py`, `git_commit.py`, `reply_comments.py`, `scripts/check_key_facts.py`, `scripts/list_all_published_titles.py`, `scripts/score_published.py`).
- Tags: mcp, python, debugging, devtools. Source: `drafts/audit-matched-drafted-codes-against-thread-roots-only.md`. Code changes: `reply_comments.py` (`_walk_comments()`, `audit()` now walks the full subtree, new selftest case); `docs/project_notes/bugs.md` (new entry).

### 2026-08-15 - DEV.to: 2 articles published (second run of the day)

- **Status**: Completed (both live; verified via HTTP 200 fetch of each URL)
- **Description**: Quota check via `/api/articles/me/published?per_page=30` showed 2 published today already (this morning's `update_article`-confirm-gate / `bugs.md`-checker pair), under the 5/day cap and non-zero, so the "still 0 on second run → at least 2" floor didn't apply — published 2 more per the normal 1-3 range, landing today's total at 4. Scored trending dev.to ai/llm/mcp/claudecode/agents/productivity posts by reactions + 3×comments. The top scorers ("The End of Undetectable AI Text? Claude's New Watermark Explained" 255, "24 Cups, 36 Seats" 242, "You Don't Have an AI Problem You Have a Thinking Problem" 180) were off-lane news/discuss/community threads. The clear runtime-authorization cluster this morning's run already mined ("I Stopped Trusting AI Agents With Tools. So I Built a Gatekeeper." 177, "Agent Plugins Package Capabilities... Who Authorizes Them at Runtime?" 95) was exhausted by today's first run's `confirm`-gate article. "A Second Brain Your AI Agent Can Read: Obsidian + Claude" (95) was likewise already used this morning for the `bugs.md`-checker article. Given how exhaustively this small codebase has been mined (130+ posts; verified via a full read of `issues.md` and `bugs.md`, not just this run's own memory), delegated two sequential fresh-angle audits to subagents — run one at a time, not in parallel, to avoid concurrent working-tree edits — each given the complete exhausted-veins list plus this morning's own new `update_article` confirm-gate/fingerprint code as additional off-limits territory, instructed to verify any finding live against the real functions (this sandbox has working `DEV_TO_API`/`GITHUB_TOKEN` credentials) and fix it for real before reporting. Both surfaced genuinely fresh, independently verified findings, both inside `update_article` — the same tool this morning's run already touched twice, but through two paths neither prior fix (nor anything before it) ever exercised:
  1. The confirm gate shipped this morning (bugs.md, this date, second entry) stops an *unconfirmed* write to a published article, but `before` is re-fetched fresh on every call, including the confirmed one — the evidence a live edit landed in between was already there, just never compared against anything. Surfaced by a real reader comment on this morning's own article (`mads_hansen_27b33ebfee4c9`, comment `3d3hj`, same day): "the preview is computed from version A, another editor changes the article to B, and the agent then sends the approved write... the diff a person reviewed is no longer necessarily the write that lands." Reproduced live with a stubbed `_dev()`: preview → simulated out-of-band edit → the originally-approved `confirm=True` call still fired and silently clobbered the intervening change. Fixed: added `_article_fingerprint()` (hash of `title`+`body_markdown`) returned on preview; an optional `expected_fingerprint` param on the confirmed call refuses the write as stale if the live article moved since preview. Deliberately opt-in — omitting it preserves the exact behavior shipped this morning, since a caller that never previewed and is knowingly forcing a value is a legitimate different case. 4 new selftest cases added (stale-blocks-zero-PUTs, fresh-fingerprint-applies, backward-compat no-fingerprint-still-applies). All 6 selftest-bearing scripts still pass.
  2. `create_article`'s duplicate-published-title guard (issues.md 2026-08-03) only ever runs on its own codepath — a fresh POST with `published=True` — and is skipped entirely for drafts by design. `update_article` can reach the identical live outcome (this account showing two published articles with the same title) two other ways neither guard nor anything else ever covered: publishing a draft via `update_article(id, published=True)` whose title happens to collide with a different already-published article, or renaming an already-published article onto another live article's exact title. Reproduced live with a stubbed `_dev()`: a draft whose title matched a different, already-published article's title was published via `update_article(id, published=True)` with zero duplicate-title check anywhere in the call trace (one GET, one PUT, no `/articles/me/published` lookup at all) — the exact outcome `create_article`'s own guard exists to prevent, reached through a door that guard was never installed on. Fixed: added `_duplicate_published_title(article_id, final_title, final_published)`, computing the title/published state the write would actually leave behind (not what kind of call it is) and refusing outright (no confirm/fingerprint override, since there's no legitimate reason to want two of your own articles live under one title) if another article already owns that title. 3 new selftest cases (draft-publish collision refused, rename-onto-duplicate refused, self-id-match correctly not flagged); one pre-existing stub needed a small patch to answer the new lookup. All 7 selftest-bearing scripts (including `scripts/check_key_facts.py`, added this morning) still pass.
  - Distinct from every prior `update_article` fix (2026-07-27 diff/log, 2026-07-29 field coverage, 2026-08-08 empty-payload guard, this morning's confirm gate) and from `create_article`'s own duplicate guard — both findings are about a write that the confirm gate lets through cleanly, and neither the diff/log nor the confirm gate nor create_article's own check has ever addressed either failure mode. Not a rehash of the idempotency-race vein either (2026-08-14, flagged not fixed) — that's a TOCTOU race on a single call path with no guard at all across two concurrent executions; both of today's findings are about a single call path that runs sequentially and correctly, just checking the wrong (or no) condition.
  - Tags: mcp, security, python, debugging; mcp, python, devtools, debugging. Sources: `drafts/confirm-gate-checked-intent-not-staleness.md`, `drafts/create-articles-duplicate-guard-had-a-second-door.md`. Code changes: `server.py` (`_article_fingerprint()` + `expected_fingerprint` param + staleness check on `update_article`; `_duplicate_published_title()` + duplicate-title check on `update_article`; both with new selftest cases).

Scored trending dev.to ai/llm/mcp/claudecode/agents/productivity posts by reactions + 3×comments. Top scorers formed a clear cluster around runtime authorization for agent tools — "I Stopped Trusting AI Agents With Tools. So I Built a Gatekeeper." (131), "Agent Plugins Package Capabilities. IRC-A Asks: Who Authorizes Them at Runtime?" (92), "I Gave My Agent One Signed Permission It Couldn't Mint Itself" (44) — plus "A Second Brain Your AI Agent Can Read: Obsidian + Claude" (95, productivity). Checked both against the full 130-title published history (`scripts/list_all_published_titles.py`) before writing anything, since two obvious first guesses for grounding the authorization cluster — "My CLAUDE.md Calls Its Routing Rules 'Mandatory.' The MCP Server Behind Them Was Never Attached." and "My CLAUDE.md Has Been Tracked and Gitignored Since the Commit That Created It" — turned out to already be published (2026-07-31, 2026-08-01). Found two angles the catalog hadn't covered and shipped real, verified code fixes for both, not just writeups:
  1. Runtime-authorization angle, grounded in `server.py`'s `update_article` tool: its 2026-07-27 fetch-before-write diff and audit log (both already covered by prior articles) record that a write happened, after it already happened — nothing upstream of the PUT ever asked whether it should. Contrasted against `_gh()`'s unconditional read-only guard (2026-08-11, already published) protecting the over-scoped `GITHUB_TOKEN` — `_dev()` had no equivalent for DEV.to writes, because `create_article`/`update_article` legitimately need to write sometimes, which had been silently treated as "write whenever asked." Fixed: added `confirm: bool = False` to `update_article`, gating only writes that would change `title`/`body_markdown` on an article the API reports as already `published` (DEV.to has no version history — that overwrite is irreversible). Unconfirmed calls return the proposed diff with `applied: False` and never reach the network; drafts and `published`-flag-only toggles are never gated, since neither is irreversible. Added 3 selftest cases (unconfirmed blocks, confirmed applies, draft bypasses the gate) verified live against a stubbed `_dev()`; `server.py --selftest` passes in full (verified via the documented FastMCP-stub-on-PYTHONPATH technique).
  2. "Second brain" angle, grounded in this project's own memory system: `scripts/check_key_facts.py` validates `key_facts.md` (missing/phantom scripts) and `decisions.md` (phantom files, ADR-001-scoped since the 2026-08-12 fix) but had never been extended to `bugs.md` — unlike `issues.md`'s exclusion, which is a documented decision (ADR-005), `bugs.md`'s absence from the checker was never a decision at all, just never added. `bugs.md` is the one file `CLAUDE.md`'s protocol says to search *first*. Manually ran the check `bugs.md` never had against the real file before writing anything: 4 filename-shaped hits (`post_article.py`, `update_profile.py`, `template.md`, `stop-hook-git-check.sh`), all independently verified legitimate (one real-and-removed, two ADR-001 phantoms already explained in `decisions.md`, one a global `~/.claude/` hook outside repo scope) — reported honestly as "currently accurate, but nothing was checking" rather than overstating it as a live bug caught. Fixed: added `bugs_phantom_files()` (same shape as the `decisions.md` version, unconditional allowlist rather than ADR-001-style section-scoping, since `bugs.md`'s historical names aren't confined to one section — documented as an accepted, narrower-safety tradeoff in code comments) and wired it into `main()`. Added 3 fixture selftest cases plus one assertion that `bugs_phantom_files()` returns empty against the real, current `bugs.md`, not just fixtures. `scripts/check_key_facts.py --selftest` and a plain run both pass; `publish_devto.py --selftest` and `git_commit.py --selftest` still pass (unaffected, but rerun to confirm no sideways breakage from a memory-system change).

Not fixed: a local lock file would be a no-op given this pipeline's fresh-container-per-session architecture (no shared filesystem across overlapping runs); a real fix needs either scheduler-level serialization or a server-side idempotency key, neither of which this script can add on its own. (overlaps the 2026-07-14 tests-pass post), "Introducing Kiro Crew" (overlaps the 2026-06-29 multi-agent post), "The Channel Gap..." (considered and dropped 2026-08-09), "A Second Brain Your AI Agent Can Read" / "The Mechanical vs. The Semantic..." (both overlap the heavily-mined memory-file vein)). No trending item cleared the distinct-angle bar, so per the quality bar, delegated two sequential fresh-angle code/docs audits to subagents (run one at a time, not in parallel, to avoid concurrent working-tree edits), each given this log's complete mined-veins list (all 120 prior titles) and instructed to verify any finding live and fix it for real before reporting, and to report "no distinct finding" rather than force something thin. Both surfaced genuinely fresh, independently verified findings:
  1. `reply_comments.py`'s `pending()` (built on `needs_reply()`/`latest_message()`) only ever surfaced the single message with the latest timestamp anywhere in a top-level comment's subtree — correct for a strictly linear thread (the only shape every prior fix and selftest case in this file modeled: 2026-08-01's nested-reply fix, 2026-08-02's root-vs-latest dedup-key fix, 2026-08-09's HTML-entity fix), but wrong the moment a thread *branches*: two different commenters replying to the same parent as siblings. Verified live with a constructed thread (root → my reply → two siblings from different users, different timestamps) using the real functions, not stubs: only the later sibling's comment ever surfaced; the earlier one — an equally real, unanswered question from a different person — silently never appeared, and became permanently unreachable once a reply to the later sibling made it the new "latest message" for the whole thread. Distinct from both prior bugs in this same tree-walking code: the 2026-08-01 fix was about reply *depth* (one level vs. all levels), the 2026-08-02 fix was about a dedup key drifting over a single chain growing over *time* — neither ever modeled two branches open *at the same time*. Fixed: added `_pending_leaves()`/`_pending_entries()`, which enumerate every unanswered leaf in a subtree instead of picking one global winner; `pending()` now iterates all of them per thread. `needs_reply()`/`latest_message()` left unchanged — still correct for the specific question they answer. Reran every existing selftest scenario against the new code path (a linear thread only ever has one leaf, so all produced identical results); added a new branching-thread regression case plus a partial-drafting case. Logged in `bugs.md` before drafting.

### 2026-08-12 - DEV.to: 2 articles published (first run of the day)

- **Status**: Completed (both live; verified via HTTP 200 fetch of each URL, with the `Mozilla/5.0` User-Agent workaround for dev.to's anonymous-fetch 403 — see `bugs.md` 2026-07-25)
- **Description**: Quota check via live API (30 most recent published) showed 0 published today, so this run published 2 per the normal 1-3 range (also satisfies the "still 0 on second run → at least 2" floor, in case this is that run). Scored trending dev.to ai/llm/mcp/claudecode/agents/productivity posts by reactions + 3×comments; nothing in the top scorers cleared the distinct-angle bar against this account's ~118-post history (same recycled off-lane discuss/community threads seen in recent runs). Delegated a full fresh-angle code audit to a subagent, given this log's complete mined-veins list and instructed to read every relevant source file plus `bugs.md`/`decisions.md`/`key_facts.md` in full before proposing anything, and to actually reproduce and fix whatever it found rather than just describe it. It surfaced two genuinely new, independently-verified findings, both closed with real fixes before drafting:
  1. `load_env()` exists as four separate copies in this repo (`server.py`, `publish_devto.py`, `reply_comments.py`, `scripts/list_all_published_titles.py`) since there's no `python-dotenv` dependency. All four ran `k, v = line.split("=", 1)` and only ever stripped `v` (three of the four already strip quotes off it; `server.py`'s copy didn't even do that). None stripped `k`. A `.env` line with spaces around `=` (`DEV_TO_API = value`, an unremarkable human-typed style) made `k` come out as `"DEV_TO_API "` — trailing space baked into the environment variable's actual name — so `os.environ.setdefault("DEV_TO_API ", ...)` set a key nothing looks up, while every real caller's `os.environ.get("DEV_TO_API")` kept returning whatever was already there. Reproduced live against this sandbox's real, already-injected `GITHUB_TOKEN`: a spaced `.env` line left the real token untouched and created a second, dead `"GITHUB_TOKEN "` key next to it — no error, no warning, the `.env` file's content silently discarded. Worse than the already-fixed missing-credential `KeyError` (`bugs.md` 2026-08-09), which at least failed loudly. Fixed: `k.strip()` added alongside the existing `v` stripping in all four files (and quote-stripping added to `server.py`'s copy, which never had it); regression cases added to each file's `--selftest` block, all passing.
  2. `_STRIP_RE` (identical copy in `server.py` and `git_commit.py`), the safety-net regex that strips AI-attribution phrasing from `claude -p`'s commit-message output, had three prior hardening passes (2026-07-22, 2026-07-26, 2026-07-29) — all in the false-positive direction (legitimate commits about "llm"/"claude" getting wiped). Nobody had checked the opposite direction. Verified directly against the compiled regex: `Signed-off-by: Claude <noreply@anthropic.com>`, `Reviewed-by: Claude <...>`, `Assisted-by: AI`, `Co-Developed-By: Claude`, and the bare literal `noreply@anthropic.com` all matched `None` — standard git trailer conventions (DCO sign-off, kernel-style review credit) sailing through a filter built entirely around the one `Co-Authored-By:` spelling this project's own incidents happened to use. Fixed: two new patterns added to both files' `_STRIP_PATTERNS`, one anchored to the trailer *value* (not just the label, to avoid reintroducing the exact false-positive class the 2026-07-22 fix already cleaned up) covering `signed-off-by`/`reviewed-by`/`acked-by`/`tested-by`/`reported-by`/`co-developed-by`/`assisted-by`, one for the literal `noreply@anthropic.com` address. Regression cases added for both the bypasses and a benign-vocabulary-overlap set (`"docs: add reviewed-by field..."` still survives); both files' `--selftest` pass.
  - Both findings logged in `docs/project_notes/bugs.md` (2026-08-12, two entries) before drafting. Distinct from every prior post on this repo's credential-loading and attribution-filter code: the `load_env` posts covered a cwd-resolution bug and a missing-credential `KeyError`, never the key/value parsing itself; the attribution-filter posts were all about narrowing over-matching, never about what the filter fails to catch.
  - Tags: python, security, debugging, devtools; ai, python, git, security. Sources: `drafts/env-loader-never-stripped-the-key.md`, `drafts/attribution-filter-only-caught-one-trailer-spelling.md`.
  - https://dev.to/enjoy_kumawat/a-space-before-the-in-my-env-file-made-a-credential-silently-disappear-3im8
  - https://dev.to/enjoy_kumawat/my-commit-message-filter-only-knew-one-way-to-say-written-by-claude-3l9b

Scored trending dev.to ai/llm/mcp/claudecode/agents/productivity posts by reactions + 3×comments; the top scorers were the same recycled set this log has excluded on every run this week ("How would you decide, whether the content is good or bad?", "We're Giving AI Agents More Tools...", "Understanding Over Origin", "Stratagems #21", "dev.to's Dashboard Can't Count Its Own Posts" (mined 2026-08-04), "The Review Tax...", "I Built an Agent Eval Harness..."). A few new entries in this run's pull ("Introducing Kiro Crew", "When Better Models Make Old Agent Workflows Worse", "The Channel Gap: Why Your LLM Judge is Blind in One Eye", "Building an AI-native Second Brain with Multi-RAG...") were checked and dropped: Kiro Crew overlaps the 2026-06-29 "One Agent or Five?" post, the rest are opinion/discussion pieces about other people's projects with no repo-groundable angle. No trending item cleared the distinct-angle bar, so per the quality bar, delegated two parallel code audits to subagents (each given this log's full mined-veins history and instructed to verify any finding live before reporting). Both surfaced genuinely fresh, independently re-verified findings; both fixes were committed and pushed before drafting:
  1. `reply_comments.py`'s `strip_html()` stripped HTML tags via regex but never decoded HTML entities. dev.to's rendered `body_html` entity-escapes a commenter's own literal `<`, `>`, `&`, and quote characters right alongside real markup tags — a comment like "isn't it faster with a Q&A cache? Try List<String>" came back through `pending()`'s `body` field as the literal `"isn&#39;t it faster with a Q&amp;A cache? Try List&lt;String&gt;"`, the exact text a reply gets drafted from. Never individually audited before — every prior comment-pipeline fix covered thread-depth walking, dedup keying, or missing `page` params, never the text-extraction step itself. Verified live: `strip_html("<p>List&lt;String&gt; and Q&amp;A, isn&#39;t it?</p>")` returned the still-escaped string before the fix, `"List<String> and Q&A, isn't it?"` after; round-tripped through `_pending_entry()`'s real `body` field, not just the standalone helper. Fixed: wrap the tag-stripped text in `html.unescape()` before whitespace collapsing (order matters — unescaping before stripping tags would let a comment quoting literal `&lt;script&gt;` decode into something the tag-stripper then eats). Added two `--selftest` cases; `reply_comments.py --selftest` passes.
     - Tags: python, debugging, devtools, mcp. Source: `drafts/comment-reply-strip-html-entities.md`. Code changes: `reply_comments.py` (`strip_html()` unescapes entities, new `--selftest` cases), `docs/project_notes/bugs.md` (new entry).
     - https://dev.to/enjoy_kumawat/my-comment-reply-pipeline-was-feeding-me-garbled-html-entities-instead-of-the-actual-comment-g43
  2. `server.py`'s `_gh()`/`_dev()` and `publish_devto.py`'s `main()` all read their API key via bare `os.environ['GITHUB_TOKEN']` / `os.environ["DEV_TO_API"]` — a missing/unset credential (no `.env` in a fresh container, a typo'd key name) raised an unhandled `KeyError` instead of the repo's own established `RuntimeError`/`ERROR:`-prefixed convention. This exact gap was named in this log's 2026-08-05 entry ("left for another run") and re-confirmed still open by a 2026-08-06 comment-reply audit, but neither mention ever became a code change — `publish_devto.py`'s identical instance (the script the scheduled task calls directly, twice a day) wasn't even named in either prior mention. Distinct from every prior missing-except-block post (those covered network-call error handling; this is the credential read itself, before any request is built) and from the twin-function-drift vein specifically applied to a bug that had been *described twice* without ever being fixed. Verified live: `env -u DEV_TO_API python3 publish_devto.py <file>.md` raised a raw `KeyError` traceback before the fix, a clean `ERROR: DEV_TO_API not set` exit after; unsetting both vars and calling `_gh`/`_dev` went from `KeyError` to `RuntimeError` naming the missing var. Fixed: both files now check `os.environ.get(...)` and fail through their own established error convention before building any request. Added regression cases to both files' `--selftest` blocks; all four self-tested scripts in this repo still pass.
     - Tags: python, debugging, mcp, devtools. Source: `drafts/credential-keyerror-flagged-not-fixed.md`. Code changes: `server.py` (`_gh`/`_dev` raise `RuntimeError` on missing credentials), `publish_devto.py` (`main()` exits via `ERROR:` on missing `DEV_TO_API`), `docs/project_notes/bugs.md` (new entry).

A call supplying none of them — `update_article(article_id=123)`, reachable by a caller that forgets an argument or misnames one — fetched the article's current state, then PUT an empty `{"article": {}}` to a live post, and logged a `fields_changed: []` entry indistinguishable from an intentional no-op. Distinct from both prior fixes to this exact function (2026-07-27 added fetch-before-write + logging so a bad write leaves a trace; 2026-07-29 made the diff cover only the fields actually written) — neither ever asked whether the function should act at all when given nothing to act on; this is an input-validation gap upstream of both. Verified live with a stubbed `_dev`: unfixed code made 1 GET + 1 PUT with an empty payload and wrote a no-op audit-log entry; fixed code raises `ValueError` before either network call, zero requests made. Fixed: guard on `if not article: raise ValueError(...)`, moved before the fetch-before-write GET so the wasted read is avoided too, not just the write. `server.py --selftest` can't run end-to-end in this sandbox (no `mcp` package, pre-existing documented limitation) — verified via a stubbed `FastMCP` shim on `PYTHONPATH` instead, same technique the 2026-08-07 audit used.

  2. `publish_devto.py`'s `already_published()` — the idempotency guard added 2026-08-03 specifically to survive a POST whose success ack gets lost to a network error — caught both `HTTPError` and `URLError` on its own verification GET and returned `None` ("can't verify, fall through to normal publish") on either. `URLError` is exactly the failure class the function's own docstring says it exists to survive; failing open on it defeats the entire guard at the one moment it matters. Separately, this log's own 2026-08-07 entry (bugs.md, the `reply_comments.py` pagination fix) falsely claimed `already_published()` already passed `page` explicitly — it never did, checking only the newest 30 of 104 published titles. Distinct from every prior missing-except-block post (both exceptions were already caught here, this is about what the catch *does*, not whether it exists) and from the pagination-truncation vein on its own (that's real but secondary; the fail-open behavior is the actual new angle, and it's the first time this log's own prior entry about a *different* function turned out to be wrong about a function it merely mentioned in passing). Verified live: stubbed `urlopen` to always raise a bare `URLError`, ran the real `main()` end-to-end against a real draft — `already_published()` returned `None`, `main()` proceeded to POST, 1 live publish went out under the exact ambiguous-network condition the guard was built to stop. Fixed: `URLError` now raises `RuntimeError` (caught in `main()` and exited through the script's existing `ERROR:` convention) instead of returning `None`; `HTTPError` still falls through (a real HTTP response is a definite, not ambiguous, "nothing to verify against"). Added `page` pagination walking to an empty page, mirroring `scripts/list_all_published_titles.py`. Reran both repros against the fixed code — `RuntimeError` raised with zero POSTs on the `URLError` case; a stubbed 3-page fixture (30 old titles, target on page 2, empty page 3) correctly found the target instead of missing it. Also corrected the false claim in `bugs.md`'s 2026-08-07 entry with an inline correction note, same pattern as ADR-005/ADR-001's corrections in `decisions.md`. `publish_devto.py --selftest` still passes.
     - Tags: python, api, debugging, devtools. Source: `drafts/idempotency-guard-fails-open-on-its-own-error.md`. The README's first-listed, "recommended" install method (`core.hooksPath` pointed straight at the tracked `hooks/` dir, no copy step) has therefore never actually invoked the hook: git silently refuses to run a non-executable hook file, a gate that runs before any of the script's own logic. Distinct from all three prior fixes to this exact file (2026-07-26 never-installed, 2026-07-31 wrong `..` depth, 2026-08-05 broke-the-other-install-method) — every one of those diagnosed and fixed `$0`-relative path arithmetic and "verified live" by manually invoking the script with a replicated cwd/`$0`, never by running an actual `git commit` and letting git decide whether to invoke the hook at all. The other documented install method (`scripts/install-hooks.sh`) masked this for months by unconditionally `chmod +x`-ing its copy regardless of the source file's tracked mode. Verified live in a scratch clone: `core.hooksPath` set per the README's Method 1 plus a real `git commit` printed `hook was ignored because it's not set as executable` and aborted on an empty message before this fix; an identical repro against the fixed tree ran the hook correctly. Fixed: `chmod +x hooks/prepare-commit-msg`, pure mode change, no content touched. All five affected scripts' `--selftest`/checks still pass.
     - Tags: python, git, debugging, devtools. Source: `drafts/hook-fix-verified-by-hand-not-by-git.md`. Code changes: `hooks/prepare-commit-msg` (mode 100644 → 100755), `docs/project_notes/bugs.md` (new entry).

It surfaced two genuinely fresh, verified findings, both independently re-verified against the live API/selftest before publishing rather than trusted on the subagent's report alone:
  1. `reply_comments.py`'s `pending()`/`audit()` fetched the account's own articles via `api(f"/articles?username={ME}&per_page=100")` with no `page` parameter — on paper safe, since the account holds exactly 100 published articles and `per_page=100` is DEV.to's own page-size ceiling. Distinct from the 2026-08-04 "Step 1 returns 30 titles not the full list" post (that was a plain page-size-vs-total arithmetic gap on a different endpoint, `/articles/me/published` — the numbers simply didn't add up) — this is the *same* endpoint, `per_page`, and expected total all checking out on paper, and only diffing two otherwise-identical live requests exposed that omitting `page` doesn't behave like `page=1`. Practical effect verified live: a real pending comment on one of this morning's two new articles was invisible to `pending()` before the fix (that article was never even in the fetched set) and correctly surfaced after it. Fixed: new `my_articles()` walks `page` explicitly from 1 until an empty page, mirroring `scripts/list_all_published_titles.py`'s existing pattern; also closes a near-miss flagged and deliberately deferred on 2026-08-04 (the account approaching the 100-per-page ceiling within about a week) as a side effect. `reply_comments.py --selftest` now covers the pagination-walk contract with a stubbed `api()`.

  2. `server.py`'s `list_repos` docstring claims `limit: 1-100`, but only the upper bound was ever enforced (`min(limit, 100)`, added for the 2026-08-06 `sort=stars`/`forks` fix) — a negative `limit` fed straight into Python's own negative-index slicing (`repos[:-1]` means "all but the last item," not "zero items"), silently returning almost the entire repo list instead of an error or an empty one. Verified live with a stubbed `_gh` returning 5 fake repos: `limit=-1` returned 4 of 5, `limit=-3` returned 2 of 5, on both the plain and `sort=stars`-ranked branches. Distinct from the 2026-08-06 `sort=` fix in this same function (that required checking GitHub's actual API reference, since code and docstring agreed with each other and were both simply wrong about what the endpoint supports) — this bug never needed an external reference at all; the gap is purely in the seam between a docstring naming an inclusive range and code enforcing only one side of it, resolved entirely by Python's own well-defined but non-obvious slice semantics. Fixed: `limit = max(0, min(limit, 100))` clamps both directions before either branch runs; `limit<=0` now correctly returns `[]` on both branches. Verified live: malformed-frontmatter draft run through the actual CLI produced a raw Python traceback, not an `ERROR:` line. Checked against GitHub's own REST API reference (via WebSearch, since direct GitHub API calls are proxy-blocked in this sandbox per `bugs.md` 2026-07-22 — confirmed still true) rather than trusting that code and docs agreeing with each other meant either was right: this endpoint's `sort` parameter only accepts `created|updated|pushed|full_name`; `stars`/`forks` are real GitHub API sort values but only on the separate `/search/repositories` endpoint, which this tool never calls. `sort` was interpolated into the query string with zero validation — GitHub silently ignores an unrecognized value and falls back to its own default, no error, nothing distinguishing "sorted as requested" from "request ignored." Distinct from every prior GitHub-helper article (those covered `_gh`'s read-only guard, missing exception handling, and per_page/pagination — never a tool's claimed *parameter semantics* against the actual API contract) and from the CLAUDE.md/MCP-security/tool-schema-bloat veins (this isn't about routing rules, prompt injection, or context cost — it's a single tool's docstring making a factual claim about a third-party API that was never true). Fixed: validate `sort` against the real API enum (fall back to `updated`), and when `stars`/`forks` is requested, fetch up to GitHub's own `per_page=100` ceiling and rank client-side against the *full* fetched set before applying `limit` — verified this ranking-before-truncation logic against an offline stub (`limit=2, sort=stars` on 5 stubbed repos correctly returns the top 2 by `stargazers_count`, not the top 2 of whichever repos happened to come back first). Could not verify the query-string/API-enum half against the live API from this sandbox (GitHub egress blocked here) — that fact rests on GitHub's published docs, not a request run from this session; flagged as the one part of the fix not closed out end-to-end.
     - Tags: mcp, python, debugging, devtools. Source: `drafts/list-repos-sort-stars-forks-github-never-supported.md`. Code changes: `server.py` (`list_repos`), `README.md` (tools table entry).
     - https://dev.to/enjoy_kumawat/my-mcp-tools-docstring-promised-sortstars-githubs-api-was-never-going-to-honor-that-value-258b

No trending item cleared the distinct-angle bar, so per the task's quality bar, audited this repo's own state instead of only its code — found one genuinely fresh, verified finding, distinct from every prior post (all 91 of which audited this repo's *code*, never a previously-*published article's own content*):
  1. "I Fixed the 'AI Commit Messages' Problem in 20 Lines of Python" (published 2026-06-21, the account's second-ever post, still live) links to `https://github.com/enjoy-kumawat/my-git-manager` — a hyphenated username that has never existed; the real one, per `key_facts.md`, is `enjoykumawat`. The typo lives in `article_draft.md`, the local source for that specific article, and has been there since before `post_article.py` (the script that originally posted it) was removed 2026-07-16. Distinct from the already-published "Username Underscore Mismatch" bug (2026-06-21, ADR-003) — that one was `.env`'s `GITHUB_USERNAME` holding the DEV.to-style underscored value, fixed by hardcoding a constant `_gh()`/`publish_devto.py` read from; this is a *third* spelling, hand-typed directly into published prose, a place no code path or that earlier fix ever touches. Verified via `git ls-remote` (not `curl` — confirmed live that GitHub's anti-bot layer 403s real repos too, e.g. `modelcontextprotocol/python-sdk`, so an HTTP status alone proves nothing): the hyphenated URL fails identically to a deliberately-fake control repo (`fatal: could not read Username...`, exit 128, reproduced 3x); the correct URL resolves cleanly every time. Fixed live via a direct `PUT` to the article's `body_markdown` (same shape `update_article` implements) and in `article_draft.md`. Then pulled every GitHub link across all 92 published articles to check scope — exactly 3 distinct links total, all resolving after the fix; this was the only dead one. Caught and reverted a false positive in the same pass before it went out wrong: initially also "fixed" the account's *first* article's link to `github.com/enjoykumawat/developer-presence-mcp` (looked like a pre-rename dead repo name, same misleading 403 pattern) — `git ls-remote` showed a real SHA and a real, matching README (a genuine, separate, still-live repo, apparently never deleted when this project's current repo took over), so reverted that `PUT` back to the original text before writing anything up. Full details: `docs/project_notes/bugs.md` 2026-08-05.
  - Also fixed, not written up (too adjacent to the 4 prior hook-path posts to count as a distinct article, but a real, verified regression): `hooks/prepare-commit-msg`'s 2026-08-04 fix (`git rev-parse --show-toplevel`) was verified only by testing `core.hooksPath` pointed at *this* repo's own tracked `hooks/` dir while committing *inside* this same repo. README's "recommended" install method sets `core.hooksPath` *globally* ("Apply to all repos on this machine") — committing in any other repo on the machine makes `--show-toplevel` resolve to that other repo, not this one, so it silently can't find `git_commit.py` and produces no message, defeating the headline use case of the method the 2026-08-04 fix was specifically written to support. Verified live: real global `core.hooksPath` pointed at a clone of this repo, committed inside a second, unrelated repo — unfixed hook produced an empty commit message file; fixed hook (branches on whether `$0` resolves under some repo's `.git/hooks/`, vs. a tracked `hooks/` dir reached via `core.hooksPath`) correctly found and ran the first repo's `git_commit.py`, producing a real `claude -p`-generated message. Reran all three scenarios (global-hooksPath-cross-repo, global-hooksPath-same-repo, copy-into-`.git/hooks`) against the fix — all three now resolve correctly.
  - Tags: debugging, python, devtools, productivity. Source: `drafts/first-articles-github-link-has-been-404ing-since-day-one.md`. Code changes: `article_draft.md` (typo fix, matches the live article), `hooks/prepare-commit-msg` (SCRIPT resolution now branches on install method instead of a single formula for both).

Quota check via live API (with the `User-Agent: Mozilla/5.0` header — bare `urllib` gets a 403 "Forbidden Bots" from dev.to itself, confirmed live) showed 0 published today; today's only prior log entry was a comment-reply-drafting run, a separate task, so this was the first article-publishing run of the day. Ranked trending dev.to ai/llm/mcp/claudecode/agents/productivity posts by reactions + 3×comments: top scorers were "Slopsquatting..." (already mined), "Skills vs MCP: How AI tools have evolved" (checked repeatedly across many prior runs — video-recap, off-voice), "Why Agent Evaluation Is Harder Than Model Evaluation" (checked previously — opinion piece about a separate project, off-voice), "Your AI Agents Need Finite State Machines" (already mined), "Understanding Over Origin"/"Vibe Coding: Endgame" (opinion pieces, no repo-groundable claim). No trending item cleared the distinct-angle bar, matching this repo's established pattern, so audited the repo's own current code instead — found two genuinely fresh, verified findings, both confirmed with real reproductions and fixed:
  1. `scripts/sync-main.sh`'s detached-HEAD recovery only tested `git merge-base --is-ancestor main "$head_sha"` (is the detached commit ahead of `main`). Anything failing that check — including the mirror-image case where `head_sha` is *behind* `main`, equally producible by this repo's own documented pinned-SHA-checkout root cause — fell straight into "needs manual review," exit 1. Verified live in a scratch repo (commit A, commit B on `main`, HEAD detached back at A): the ahead-check correctly returned false, while a second check (`is-ancestor head_sha main`) confirmed `head_sha` was fully contained in `main` — completely safe to just check out, no merge needed. In an unattended scheduled session "needs manual review" is a stall, not a review. Fixed: added an `elif` branch for the behind case (`git checkout main` directly, no merge). Reran the repro against the fixed script — correctly checks out `main` without attempting a merge.
  2. `hooks/prepare-commit-msg`'s 2026-07-31 path-depth fix (`dirname "$0")/../..`) was verified only against `scripts/install-hooks.sh`'s copy-into-`.git/hooks/` method. `README.md`'s own "Pre-commit hook (auto mode)" section documents a second method as the *first*, "recommended" option — `git config --global core.hooksPath <repo>/hooks`, invoking the hook straight from its tracked location, one level shallower than the copied version. Verified live: real git repo, `core.hooksPath` set to the tracked `hooks/` dir, hook invoked exactly as git would (cwd = repo root, real args) — the two-`..` `SCRIPT` path resolved one directory above the actual project root, `git_commit.py` not found there (confirmed via `ls`, not a downstream `claude`-not-found error). Re-verified the copy-into-`.git/hooks/` method still needs two `..`s, confirming the two documented install methods are irreconcilable with any single `dirname "$0")`-based depth. Fixed by dropping `$0`-relative arithmetic entirely: `SCRIPT="$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)/git_commit.py"`. Reran both install methods against the fix — both resolve correctly.

Sources: `drafts/sync-main-only-handled-head-ahead-of-main.md`, `drafts/hook-path-fix-broke-the-other-install-method.md`. Code changes: `scripts/sync-main.sh` (new `elif` branch handles HEAD behind `main` with a plain checkout), `hooks/prepare-commit-msg` (`SCRIPT` now resolves via `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` instead of `$0`-relative arithmetic).

Quota check via live API showed 2 published today (this morning's `reply_comments.py`/`publish_devto.py` except-block pair), under the 5/day cap and non-zero, so the "second run, still 0 → publish at least 2" floor didn't apply — normal 1-3 range. Ranked trending dev.to ai/llm/mcp/claudecode/agents/productivity posts by reactions + 3×comments. Every top scorer was already excluded by prior runs' extensive logs: "The Junior Developer Pipeline Is Broken..." (r259 c208), "Slopsquatting..." (already mined 2026-07-30), "Understanding Over Origin", "Vibe Coding: Endgame", "Unlimited context is not a feature" (too close to the context-flooding/context-rot posts), "If Your AI Agent Has Write Access to Public Repos" (already mined 2026-07-30), "My Verification Gate Cleared on a Keyword, Not Evidence" (self-distrust/verification vein, repeatedly flagged as overused), "Your AI Agents Need Finite State Machines" (already mined 2026-07-30), "Too many Claude Code skills?..." (skipped again — tool-schema-bloat overlap risk), "Skills vs MCP: How AI tools have evolved" (checked repeatedly — video-recap, off-voice), "My eval said a perfect MCP server was broken" (third-party eval tool, unverifiable here), "The memory layer that never calls an LLM" (cross-session-memory vein, dropped). Checked one new candidate against its actual content: "Make agent-callable writes idempotent, or lose data" (mcp, r0 c1) — rather than dismiss it on the "no distributed workers here" reflex, checked whether this repo's own single retry-by-instruction path (this task's own Step 4: "if 429, wait 35 seconds and retry") is actually safe to retry. It wasn't, and this is a genuinely fresh angle no prior post has touched — found and fixed a real, verified bug:
  1. `publish_devto.py`'s `main()` (the script this exact scheduled task calls to go live) POSTs to `/api/articles` with no idempotency guard of any kind, and DEV.to's API has no idempotency-key mechanism to lean on. Its own `except urllib.error.URLError` branch — added in this morning's run specifically to catch timeouts, DNS failures, and connection drops, not just HTTP-status errors — means the script now has a clean exit path for exactly the failure mode where success is ambiguous: a timeout on `urlopen()` doesn't distinguish "server never got the request" from "server processed it, the ack just never arrived." Verified live with an offline repro (stubbed `urlopen`: first call records the POST as server-side-succeeded, then raises `URLError("timed out")` before any response reaches the caller): first attempt exits with the timeout message, one article already recorded server-side; a second call — modeling the retry this task's own instructions would trigger on what looks like the same kind of failure — POSTs again with the identical title/body and the stub's server-side list grows to two entries. Confirmed this is distinct from every prior fix to this script (the 2026-08-01 tag-truncation fix and this morning's `HTTPError`→`URLError` fix both concern how a single request fails or is shaped, never whether a second, retried request is safe to fire) and distinct from every prior "twin function drift" post (this is the first to note that hardening an except clause to correctly catch a failure mode doesn't make retrying safe — it just makes the ambiguous case reachable via a clean exit instead of a crash). Fixed: added `already_published(key, title)`, which checks the 30 most recent entries from `/api/articles/me/published` for an exact title match before POSTing, and `main()` now short-circuits to `ALREADY PUBLISHED (skipped duplicate) -> <url>` instead of re-creating. Reran the identical repro against the fixed script — second attempt correctly detects the existing title via a GET and makes zero POST calls; the stub's server-side list stays at one entry across both attempts. Found the identical gap in `server.py`'s `create_article` MCP tool (a separate code path to the same endpoint, for a client like Claude Desktop calling this server directly) and applied the same fix there, verified with a separate offline repro asserting no POST call occurs once a matching published title is found via `_dev()`.
  - Tags: python, devtools, debugging, ai. Source: `drafts/my-publish-script-had-no-idempotency-guard-a-timeout-after-success-meant-a-live-duplicate.md`. Code changes: `publish_devto.py` (new `already_published()` helper; `main()` skips the POST and returns the existing URL when a matching published title is found), `server.py` (`create_article` gets the same duplicate-title check via `_dev()` before POSTing when `published=True`).
  - https://dev.to/enjoy_kumawat/my-publish-script-has-a-retry-instruction-in-its-own-task-prompt-it-had-no-guard-against-that-l7k

  1. `reply_comments.py`'s `api()` — the single helper backing both `pending()` and `audit()`, hit twice a day by the unattended comment-reply pipeline — had zero exception handling around its `urlopen()` call, the identical shape as `server.py`'s `_gh()`/`_dev()` bug fixed 2026-08-01 ("zero except blocks, every bad call crashed with a raw urllib traceback"). Verified live: stubbed `urlopen` to raise a 429, called `api()` — the raw `HTTPError` propagated uncaught straight out of `pending()`/`audit()`, no different from a bug in the code itself. Distinct from every prior fix to this file (three earlier rounds — `needs_reply()`'s recency bug, `audit()`'s one-level-deep walk, `pending()`'s root-keyed dedup — all touched the comment-tree-walking logic sitting *on top of* `api()`, never the network call underneath it) and distinct from the `server.py` fix itself (same bug shape, different file, written before that hardening pass and never checked against it afterward). Fixed: wrapped `urlopen()` in `try/except HTTPError`, raising a clean `RuntimeError` with status code and truncated response body, mirroring `server.py`'s exact pattern. Reran the 429 repro against the fixed code — clean `RuntimeError`, no raw traceback.
  2. `publish_devto.py`'s `main()` — the script the scheduled publishing routine (this very task) calls to go live — catches `urllib.error.HTTPError` but not the broader `urllib.error.URLError` it's a subclass of. `HTTPError` only covers responses with an actual HTTP status code; `URLError` also covers timeouts, DNS failures, and connection-refused — exactly the failure class the call's own `timeout=30` argument makes possible. Verified live: stubbed `urlopen` to raise a timeout `URLError`, ran `main()` against a real draft file — uncaught, propagated as a raw exception, no `sys.exit` message, indistinguishable from an actual bug in the script (unlike the clean, controlled exit a real `HTTPError` already got). Distinct from every prior mention of this script's error handling — its `except HTTPError` had been cited *approvingly* multiple times (most recently in 2026-08-01's `_gh`/`_dev` post) as the pattern other files should copy, but nobody had checked whether that one except clause was complete for the failure modes the call itself invites. Fixed: added `except urllib.error.URLError as e: sys.exit(f"URLError: {e.reason}")` after the `HTTPError` branch. Reran the identical timeout repro against the fixed script — clean `sys.exit` with a readable message. Did not add a typed `retryable` flag or unify this with the same open gap already logged for `server.py`'s `_gh`/`_dev` (uniform exit code across distinct HTTP failures) — same restraint as prior passes: closing the missing-branch gap fit this pass, redesigning the failure-reporting contract didn't.

Code changes: `reply_comments.py` (`api()` now catches `HTTPError` and raises a clean `RuntimeError`), `publish_devto.py` (`main()` now also catches `URLError`, not just `HTTPError`).

No trending item cleared the distinct-angle bar, so per the task's quality bar, audited this repo's own current code instead — found one genuinely fresh, verified finding, confirmed with a real reproduction and fixed:
  1. `reply_comments.py`'s `pending()` walks each article's top-level comments and, for each one needing a reply (per `needs_reply()`, itself fixed 2026-07-26 for a different bug — recency vs. ever-replied), used that top-level comment `c`'s own `id_code`/`body_html` for both the dedup check against `drafted_codes` and the content surfaced to the human pasting the reply. That's correct only for a single-round thread. In a real back-and-forth (root comment drafted and replied to, then the other person posts a genuine follow-up nested under my reply), the follow-up is a distinct comment object with its own `id_code` — but `c` in the loop is still the root, so the dedup check tests the *root's* `id_code` (already in `drafted_codes` from round one) instead of the follow-up's (never drafted, never checked), and the thread silently drops off `pending()`'s output forever after its first draft, no matter how many further rounds happen. Verified live with a constructed 3-level tree (root drafted → my reply → their follow-up, distinct `id_code`s at each level): `needs_reply(root)` correctly returned `True` (the follow-up is the latest message), but `root["id_code"] in drafted_codes` was also `True` (matching the *root's* id, already drafted in round one) — confirming the thread would vanish from `pending()`'s output even though a genuinely new, undrafted message existed three levels below the check. Distinct from the two prior fixes to this file: the 2026-07-26 `needs_reply()` fix changed *which* message counts as needing a reply (recency vs. ever-replied) inside a function that's correct now, verified by this run's own repro; the 2026-08-01 `audit()` fix was a one-level-vs-full-subtree walk depth bug in a different function entirely (checking whether a reply was ever posted, not whether one is pending). This is the first finding on the dedup *key* itself pointing at a stable node in a tree whose interesting node moves every round — and the reason it survived two rounds of fixing the file's sibling functions is that each fix made the surrounding code look more trustworthy without anyone re-checking whether the id attached to a now-correct boolean was still the right id. Fixed: added `_pending_entry(comment, drafted_codes)`, which calls the file's own existing `latest_message()` (already used inside `needs_reply()`) to find the actual unanswered message and uses *its* `id_code`/`body_html` for both the dedup check and the returned entry, at whatever depth it sits; `pending()` now calls this instead of inlining the root-keyed logic. Added a `--selftest` case reproducing the exact round-2 scenario — confirms the follow-up surfaces with its own id/body once its root has already been drafted, and correctly drops out only once the follow-up's own id gets drafted too.
  - Tags: python, debugging, devtools, ai. Source: `drafts/pending-reply-check-was-keyed-on-the-wrong-comment.md`. Code changes: `reply_comments.py` (new `_pending_entry()` helper keys `pending()`'s dedup check and returned content off `latest_message()`'s result instead of the top-level comment; `--selftest` covers the drafted-root/undrafted-follow-up case).
  - https://dev.to/enjoy_kumawat/my-comment-reply-queue-draft-one-reply-to-a-thread-and-it-went-deaf-to-every-follow-up-after-that-2dlc

No trending item cleared the distinct-angle bar, so per the task's quality bar, audited this repo's own current code instead — found two genuinely fresh, verified findings, both confirmed with real reproductions and fixed:
  1. `server.py`'s `_ARTICLE_UPDATE_LOG` (the JSONL audit log `update_article` writes to, built specifically so a bad write leaves a trace) was still declared as a bare relative string, `"logs/article_updates.jsonl"` — the identical bug shape as `load_env()`'s pre-2026-07-31 `.env` path, in the same file, nineteen lines below that fix. Verified live by pulling the exact function body out and running it standalone (this sandbox has no `mcp` package installed, so no direct import of `server.py`): simulated an MCP client launching from an arbitrary `cwd` via `os.chdir(tempfile.mkdtemp())`, called `_log_article_update`, confirmed the log landed in the scratch directory's `logs/`, not the repo's — `key_facts.md`'s documented Claude Desktop config sets `command`+`args` only, no `cwd`, matching exactly the launch shape that triggers this. Distinct from the 2026-07-31 "audit log leaves no trace" post (that found the log was never committed to git — a durability/sharing gap) and from the `.env` fix itself (a different variable in the same file) — this is the first post to note that fixing one cwd-relative-path bug in a file doesn't prompt a check of the rest of the file for the same shape, even when re-reading it minutes earlier for the adjacent fix. Fixed: `_ARTICLE_UPDATE_LOG` now resolves via `os.path.join(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)), "logs", "article_updates.jsonl")`, matching `load_env()`'s pattern. Reran the identical repro against the fixed constant — resolves to the repo's own `logs/` directory regardless of `cwd` at call time.
  2. Re-tested the 2026-07-26 attribution-filter fix (which replaced bare substring matching with anchored regexes after "llm"/"claude" as ordinary words got wrongly stripped) against a new axis it was never tested on: `\bclaude code\b`, one of the six anchored patterns, is a bare phrase match with no requirement that it appear as part of an attribution statement. Since this repo's own commit history is largely about fixing `hooks/prepare-commit-msg`/`scripts/install-hooks.sh`/the `claude -p` wrapper, legitimate one-line messages naming the tool ("docs: add claude code hook install instructions", "fix: handle claude code MCP timeout in server.py") get matched and, because the generator's system prompt guarantees exactly one output line, the per-line filter reduces the *entire* message to an empty string — no partial redaction, indistinguishable from the hook silently having nothing to say (same visible symptom as two earlier, differently-caused bug-log entries). Verified live against both the old and new regex with a candidate set of 5 realistic messages: old regex stripped 4/5 to empty; confirmed `\bclaude code\b` was not simply redundant with `generated (with|by)\s+claude` before touching it — the real Claude Code attribution footer is markdown-bracketed (`Generated with [Claude Code](url)`), which breaks the no-punctuation `generated (with|by)\s+claude` pattern, so the bare phrase match was catching a real case the other pattern missed, not dead weight. Fixed: replaced `\bclaude code\b` with `\b(with|by|using|via)\s*\[?\s*claude code\]?` in both `server.py` and `git_commit.py` (same pattern list duplicated by copy, not import — checked both since this repo has a known twin-drift history). Reran the full candidate set against the fixed regex — all 4 legitimate technical mentions now survive unstripped; the bracketed real footer, the co-author line, and "written by an AI" all still get caught.
  - Tags: mcp, python, debugging, devtools; git, ai, claudecode, debugging. Sources: `drafts/audit-log-forgot-the-cwd-fix-its-own-file-already-had.md`, `drafts/attribution-filter-blocks-legitimate-claude-code-mentions.md`. Code changes: `server.py` (`_ARTICLE_UPDATE_LOG` now resolves relative to `__file__`; `_STRIP_PATTERNS`' `\bclaude code\b` narrowed to require a preceding preposition), `git_commit.py` (same `_STRIP_PATTERNS` fix, kept in sync with `server.py`).

(skipped again — tool-schema-bloat overlap risk). Checked two borderline trending items against this repo's actual code rather than dismissing on title alone: "MCP Went Stateless: Migrating to the 2026-07-28 Spec" doesn't apply — `server.py` runs over stdio (`mcp.run()`, no port/URL, launched as a subprocess per `key_facts.md`'s Claude Desktop config), not an HTTP server, so the article's entire premise (session headers, sticky routing, load balancers) has no target here; "What actually belongs in CLAUDE.md" maps cleanly onto this repo's own routing block but critiquing `CLAUDE.md`'s content is off-limits (user's own instructions file) and would be a third angle on a file already covered twice. No trending item cleared the distinct-angle bar, so per the task's quality bar, audited this repo's own current code instead — found two genuinely fresh, verified findings, both confirmed with real reproductions and fixed:
  1. `server.py`'s two HTTP helpers, `_gh()` (GitHub) and `_dev()` (DEV.to), had zero `try/except` around their `urllib.request.urlopen()` calls — every one of the 7 tools built on them (`list_articles`, `create_article`, `update_article`, `get_article_stats`, `get_github_profile`, `list_repos`, `get_repo_stats`) let any `HTTPError` (bad `article_id`/repo name, expired token, 422, 429) propagate as a raw uncaught exception, unlike `_claude()`'s three-times-hardened `ERROR:`-prefixed convention and unlike `publish_devto.py`'s own `except urllib.error.HTTPError` around the identical call shape. Verified live with `urlopen` stubbed to raise a 404: `update_article(article_id=999999)` and `get_repo_stats('doesnotexist')` both surfaced as `UNCAUGHT HTTPError propagated to caller`, no message content, in contrast to `_claude()`'s `'ERROR: claude -p exited 1: boom'`. Distinct from the 2026-07-28 "catches timeout, not non-zero exit" and "ERROR: convention" posts — both scoped to the `claude -p` subprocess call only, never touched `_gh`/`_dev`'s HTTP failure surface. Fixed: both helpers now catch `urllib.error.HTTPError` and raise `RuntimeError(f"... API error {e.code}: {e.read().decode()[:400]}")` instead of letting the raw exception through unlabeled. Reran the stubbed-404 repro against the fixed code — clean `RuntimeError` with status code and response body, not a bare `HTTPError` repr. Also fixed, same pass: `_gh`'s read-only guard checked the verb but let a `data` payload ride along on a `GET` call unrejected (surfaced in an unactioned 2026-08-01 comment reply) — guard now also rejects any non-`None` `data` on `_gh`.
  2. `create_article` never truncated `tags` to DEV.to's 4-tag limit, while its twin `publish_devto.py` (the script the scheduled publishing routine actually calls) already does (`[...][:4]`) — same repo, same endpoint, same rule enforced in exactly one of the two places that need it. Verified this wasn't just a stylistic diff: stubbed `urlopen` to capture the outgoing payload, called `create_article` with 6 tags — all 6 went out unmodified; ran the same 6 tags through `publish_devto.py`'s truncation logic in the same session — correctly cut to 4. Confirmed the 4-tag pattern is real and load-bearing by checking this log's own history: every one of ~30 prior published articles has exactly 4 tags, never more — an undocumented constraint encoded once, in one of two article-creation code paths, never checked against the other. Distinct from prior "twin function drifted" posts (system prompt string 2026-07-29, empty-diff guard 2026-07-24, `.env` path 2026-07-31) — same recurring shape, first instance of it being a numeric limit rather than a string or guard clause. Fixed: `payload["article"]["tags"] = tags[:4]`. Reran the payload-capture repro — `create_article` now sends exactly 4 tags regardless of input length. Did not extract a shared `_normalize_tags()` helper between the two files — this fix closes the one confirmed divergence; deduplicating on a single occurrence felt premature, left as an open smell noted in the article itself. Did not independently verify DEV.to's live API response to a 5-tag POST (422 vs. silent truncation) — the fix is correct regardless (it makes both code paths agree with the repo's own established pattern), but that specific verification gap is called out explicitly in the article rather than asserted as checked.
  - Tags: mcp, python, debugging, devtools; mcp, python, devtools, ai. Sources: `drafts/gh-and-dev-helpers-had-no-http-error-handling.md`, `drafts/create-article-never-truncated-tags.md`. Code changes: `server.py` (`_gh()`/`_dev()` now catch `HTTPError` and raise clean `RuntimeError`s; `_gh()` also rejects a `data` payload on GET; `create_article` truncates `tags` to 4).

  1. Checked `.gitignore` against `git ls-files` and found `CLAUDE.md` listed as ignored (line 9) while also being fully tracked (`git ls-files CLAUDE.md` returns it). `git log --all -- CLAUDE.md` and `git log --all -- .gitignore` both point to the same origin commit (`29e0915`) — the file was added to the index and excluded by `.gitignore` in the same commit, which is why it's never actually been affected: tracked files bypass ignore rules by design. Verified the trap this leaves: `git check-ignore -v CLAUDE.md` (no flag) returns nothing/exit 1 — false reassurance, since check-ignore skips already-tracked paths — while `git check-ignore -v --no-index CLAUDE.md` correctly shows the pattern matches (`.gitignore:9:CLAUDE.md`). Reproduced the actual failure mode in a scratch clone: `git rm --cached CLAUDE.md`, delete, recreate with new content, `git add -A` — result is a staged deletion (`D  CLAUDE.md`) plus the new content sitting as untracked-and-ignored (`!! CLAUDE.md`, only visible with `git status --ignored`), never picked up by `git add -A`/`git add .` again without an explicit `-f`. Distinct from the 2026-07-31 CLAUDE.md post (that was about the routing-rules *content* asserting infrastructure that isn't attached; this is about the file's own git-tracking state being self-contradictory since its first commit, a completely different mechanism). Did not remove the `.gitignore` line or otherwise touch the file's tracking state — CLAUDE.md is the user's own instructions file, deciding its ignore/track status is their call, not a bug with an obvious one-line fix to auto-apply. Logged as a live but currently-dormant trap instead.
  2. Re-audited `reply_comments.py`'s `audit()` (unposted-reply detector, sibling to the `pending()`/`needs_reply()` pair fixed 2026-07-26) and found it checks only `c["children"]` (direct replies to the root comment) for a message from ME, while `needs_reply()`/`latest_message()` (fixed in the same 2026-07-26 pass, for a different bug) correctly recurse the whole subtree. A reply nested two levels deep — root comment → their follow-up → my reply to that follow-up, an ordinary back-and-forth shape — is invisible to `audit()`'s one-level check. Verified live with a constructed 3-level thread + a "drafted" marker for the root id_code: unfixed `audit()` reported it as `never_posted` while `needs_reply()` on the identical structure correctly returned `False`. Distinct from the 2026-07-26 fix itself (that fixed *which* message counts as "the reply" — recency vs. ever-replied — inside `needs_reply()`; this is a second function in the same file asking a simpler-sounding question, "did I reply at all," that never got the same recursive treatment, and had zero test coverage before today, unlike `needs_reply()`). Fixed: added `replied_anywhere_in_subtree()` (same recursive shape as `latest_message()`) and swapped it into `audit()` in place of the one-level `any(...)` check. Reran the identical repro against the fixed code — `never_posted` now correctly empty. Added a nested-reply case to the file's `--selftest` block, which previously only covered `needs_reply()`.
  - Tags: git, claudecode, devtools, debugging; python, debugging, devtools, ai. Sources: `drafts/claude-md-tracked-and-gitignored-same-commit.md`, `drafts/audit-only-checked-one-level-deep.md`. Code changes: `reply_comments.py` (`audit()` now uses new `replied_anywhere_in_subtree()` instead of a one-level `any()` check; `--selftest` covers a nested-reply case).

(skipped again — same tool-schema-bloat overlap risk as this morning). No trending item cleared the distinct-angle bar, so per the task's quality bar, audited this repo's own current code instead — found two genuinely fresh, verified findings, both confirmed with real reproductions rather than by rereading the code:
  1. Re-examined `hooks/prepare-commit-msg`, the subject of three prior posts (timeout handling 2026-07-23, "never installed as a real git hook" 2026-07-26, "install didn't survive a fresh container" 2026-07-27) — but none of those three ever verified the hook actually produces an AI commit message end-to-end once installed. It doesn't, and never has: `SCRIPT="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")/.." && pwd)/git_commit.py"` was written assuming `$0` resolves from this file's tracked location (`<repo>/hooks/`), where one `..` reaches the repo root. But git only ever invokes hooks from `.git/hooks/` (per `scripts/install-hooks.sh`'s flat copy) — one directory deeper — so the same one `..` lands in `.git/`, not the repo root, and `SCRIPT` points at `<repo>/.git/git_commit.py`, which has never existed in this repo's history. Verified live: copied the real working tree to a scratch clone, ran the real `scripts/install-hooks.sh`, did a real `git commit` with a working `claude` CLI on PATH (so this isn't the missing-binary case either) — `python .git/git_commit.py` fails with `FileNotFoundError`, silently eaten by the existing `2>/dev/null`, `MSG` stays empty, and `git commit` aborts on an empty message, indistinguishable from "the hook isn't installed at all." Fixed: changed to `dirname "$0")/../..` (two levels from `.git/hooks/` to repo root). Reran the identical repro against the fixed hook — a real Conventional Commit message, generated by `claude -p`, landed in the test commit for the first time since this hook was written.
  2. Audited whether the JSONL audit log the 2026-07-27 `update_article` fix added (`logs/article_updates.jsonl`, built specifically so a bad write would leave a trace) actually persists anywhere. It doesn't: `git log --all --oneline -- 'logs/*'` is empty across all 50 commits (repo not shallow, confirmed via `git rev-parse --is-shallow-repository`), `logs/` isn't in `.gitignore` either — it's simply never been part of any committed state, and doesn't exist on disk right now. Root cause: `update_article` is an MCP tool invoked wherever a client has this server wired up (per `key_facts.md`, that's Claude Desktop on the user's own machine), while the scheduled publishing routine that writes these articles is a separate, fresh-per-session cloud container that never calls `update_article` at all (it publishes via `publish_devto.py` directly) — so the one tool this log exists for writes a plain local file with no step anywhere in this repo's workflow that commits it somewhere both environments could read. Checked whether `scripts/check_key_facts.py` (built to catch exactly this class of docs/disk drift, fixed twice already for missing-from-docs and phantom-in-docs script rows) would ever catch this: it only walks `ROOT`, `scripts/`, `hooks/` for `.py`/`.sh` files — a runtime JSONL log in an undocumented `logs/` directory is categorically outside what it looks for. Did not force a fix — same restraint as the 2026-07-19 stop-hook post: committing a growing audit log forever has the same downside ADR-005 already rejected for `drafts/`, and picking the right fix (shared log location vs. explicitly scoping the tool's durability claim) is a design decision, not a five-minute patch. Logged as an open gap instead.
  - Tags: git, python, claudecode, debugging; mcp, python, devtools, debugging. Sources: `drafts/commit-hook-never-found-its-own-script.md`, `drafts/audit-log-that-leaves-no-trail.md`. Code changes: `hooks/prepare-commit-msg` (`SCRIPT` path now correctly resolves two directories up from the installed `.git/hooks/` location, not one).

  - https://dev.to/enjoy_kumawat/my-mcp-tools-audit-log-was-built-so-a-bad-write-would-leave-a-trace-the-log-itself-leaves-none-4maf

Also surfaced the one place local state *does* matter (the issues.md log entry, if a partial multi-article publish gets logged as fully successful) and pointed at Step 4's existing "verify each article is live by fetching its URL" instruction as the real state-transition guard (`attempted -> confirmed_live`) already doing that job in prose. Distinct from the FSM/verification-discipline posts already published (those were about tests/tool-results self-reporting; this is specifically about *where* durable state should live across a crash-prone unattended run).
