Quota check via live API showed 0 published today and no first-run log entry yet today, so this was the first article-publishing run of the day. Ranked trending dev.to ai/llm/mcp/claudecode/agents/productivity posts by reactions + 3×comments. Every top scorer was already excluded by prior runs' extensive logs or off-voice: "The Junior Developer Pipeline Is Broken...", "The Dirty Secret Behind AI Agents (Demo)", "The Person Who Fixed the Bugs Just Vanished" (all repeatedly flagged discuss/marketing/off-voice), "How I reduced AI coding context by 95%" (too close to the context-flooding/context-mode vein), "I Connected 3 MCP Servers to One Agent" (single-MCP-server repo, its credential-mixing reframe already mined 2026-07-28), "My AI pentest agent reported 23 root shells..." (faked-tool-result vein, already mined), "I Let an Agent Take Over an Account With Every Permission Check Green" (already mined 2026-07-27), "My Verification Gate Cleared on a Keyword, Not Evidence" (self-distrust/verification vein, repeatedly flagged as overused). One trending item did survive first-pass filtering — "If Your AI Agent Has Write Access to Public Repos, Audit It Now" (ai/security, r36 c14) — and rather than dismiss it on the "no write tools here" reflex, went and read the actual GitHub helper function instead of just the tool list. Found a real, previously-undocumented gap: `server.py`'s `_gh(path, method="GET", data=None)` is a fully generic HTTP client (accepts any verb, serializes and attaches `data` if given) — grepped every call site (`get_github_profile`, `list_repos`, `get_repo_stats`) and confirmed all three pass only the default `GET` with no `data`, so the "read-only" story was true by convention only, never enforced. That gap is sharper than it looks because of what actually backs the call: `key_facts.md` documents the real token's scope as `repo, user` — full read/write across every repo the account can reach, not read-only. The token doesn't know only reads are happening; until this run, neither did the function. Distinct from the 2026-07-28 "two API keys share one process" post (that was about *two different credentials* sharing a process, no fix shipped) and the 2026-07-22 GitHub-proxy-403 post (a sandbox artifact, different mechanism entirely) — this is about *one* credential having reach far beyond what any current tool needs, with nothing in code resisting a stray write call from a future tool, a bad edit, or an over-eager agent. Fixed this run: added a `method != "GET"` guard to `_gh` that raises `ValueError` before any request is constructed. Verified two ways with `urlopen` stubbed (no dependency on this sandbox's own known GitHub-proxy quirks): the three existing read tools still make exactly the same `GET` calls and return the same shapes; a stray `_gh(path, method="DELETE")` now raises immediately with zero network calls made, confirmed via a call counter.
- Tags: mcp, security, python, devtools. Source: `drafts/gh-helper-could-write-nothing-used-it-that-way.md`. Code changes: `server.py` (`_gh` now rejects any non-`GET` method).

The 2026-06-21 comment "fix: strip AI attribution lines from generated commit messages" (`1f8808c`) touched both `git_commit.py` and `server.py` in the same commit, but not the same way: it strengthened `git_commit.py`'s system prompt with an explicit "one line, ... no co-author lines, no signatures, no AI references" instruction, while `server.py`'s `generate_commit_message` tool only got the shared `_STRIP` filter added to `_claude()` — its own system prompt argument was never touched, and still read the old, unstrengthened text five weeks and two regex-hardening passes (2026-07-22, 2026-07-26) later. Verified via `git log --reverse -p` on both files to pin the exact fork point, then confirmed live: the shared `_STRIP_RE` regex returns `False` (i.e. does not catch) synthetic model output like `"As an AI language model, I cannot take credit for this commit."` — exactly the class of phrasing `git_commit.py`'s extra instruction is designed to prevent at the source, and which `server.py`'s tool had zero defense against beyond the same downstream regex. Distinct from the 2026-06-21/2026-07-19 "same code" framing (that was about the shared `_claude()` helper and STRIP filter being identical, which they are) and the 2026-07-22/2026-07-26 STRIP-filter posts (both about the regex itself) — this is about the instruction layer upstream of the filter, never diffed in four separate touchpoints on this exact function pair. Fixed: `server.py`'s `generate_commit_message` system prompt now matches `git_commit.py`'s verbatim. Verified via `inspect.getsource` that the string now contains the same instruction.
  2. Re-audited the 2026-07-27 `update_article` fetch-before-write fix (the one that added diff/log to close the blind-overwrite gap) and found its own diff/log only ever covered `title`/`published`, hardcoded, regardless of which field the caller actually changed — `body_markdown`, the field this tool exists to edit, was never in either. Verified offline (stubbed before/after states, no live article touched, same caution as the original fix): calling `update_article` with only `body_markdown` set produced a diff showing `title` and `published` both unchanged, with no field anywhere reflecting that the entire body had just been replaced; the JSONL log recorded `fields_changed: ["body_markdown"]` but no `body_markdown_before`/`after` values, so even the audit trail couldn't show what changed. Distinct from the 2026-07-27 post itself (found the tool had no diff/log at all) — this is the fix for that gap having a coverage gap of its own, caught only by asking which field a real call is actually likely to touch rather than trusting that a `diff` key existing meant the diff was complete. Fixed: `diff` is now built from `article.keys()` (whatever fields were actually part of the write) instead of a fixed pair, and `_log_article_update` now logs before/after only for fields in `fields_changed`, covering `body_markdown` for the first time. Reran the same stubbed scenario against the fixed code — diff and log both now show the actual old/new body text.

Code changes: `server.py` (`generate_commit_message` system prompt now matches `git_commit.py`'s; `update_article`'s `diff` and `_log_article_update` now cover only-and-all actually-changed fields, including `body_markdown`).

Re-audited `server.py` under that reframing rather than the surface one: it's one MCP server, but it loads both `GITHUB_TOKEN` and `DEV_TO_API` into the same process at import time (`load_env()`), and every one of its 8 tools — including `generate_commit_message`, which touches neither API — runs in that same process with both credentials sitting in its environment. No prior article argues the actual point here: that single-process credential mixing is the same failure shape as the trending multi-server post, just harder to see because there's no second wire to point at. Did not modify `server.py` this run — the article proposes and sketches a two-process split (separate `.env`s, separate `FastMCP` instances per credential domain) as the fix but stops short of applying it, stated as such in the piece itself.

Quota check via live API showed 0 published today (new UTC day; last entry was 2026-07-27's comment-reply run), so this was the first article-publishing run of the day; published 2 per the normal 1-3 range. Ranked trending dev.to ai/llm/mcp/claudecode/agents/productivity posts by reactions + 3×comments. Every top scorer was already excluded by prior runs' logs or off-voice: "The Dirty Secret Behind AI Agents (Demo)" (marketing-tone, excluded repeatedly), "The Person Who Fixed the Bugs Just Vanished" (no clean first-hand hook, excluded before), "The Junior Developer Pipeline Is Broken... And AI Broke It" (opinion/community-discuss shape, off-voice), "Loop Engineering: Stop Your Agent Reward-Hacking Its Own Checks" (repeatedly flagged too close to the self-distrust/verification vein), "Sentry's Span Hierarchy Exposed a Silent Retry in My 5-Agent Pipeline" (this repo runs one MCP server, not a 5-agent pipeline — no genuine angle), "I Let an Agent Take Over an Account With Every Permission Check Green" (same "check passed, outcome still bad" shape already mined for the 2026-07-27 `update_article` blind-write post). No trending item offered a genuinely distinct, repo-grounded angle, so per the task's own quality bar, went back to auditing this repo's own current code instead of forcing a trend match — found two, both verified live and fixed this run:
  1. The 2026-07-26 post ("I Gave My MCP Tool an ERROR: Convention...") fixed a mismatch between `_claude()`'s two *returning* failure paths (empty-diff guard, timeout) so both carried an `ERROR:` prefix, and treated the convention as settled. It wasn't: `subprocess.check_output(["claude", "-p", ...])` also raises `subprocess.CalledProcessError` on a non-zero exit and `FileNotFoundError` if the binary is missing from `PATH` — neither is a `TimeoutExpired`, so neither was caught by the sole `except subprocess.TimeoutExpired` clause in either `server.py`'s `_claude()` or `git_commit.py`'s inline call. Verified live: dropped a one-line stand-in `claude` binary onto `PATH` that exits 1, ran the real staged-diff path through `git_commit.py`, got a full uncaught `CalledProcessError` traceback (exit 1) instead of any clean message; repeated with `PATH` emptied of `claude` entirely and got an uncaught `FileNotFoundError` the same way; reproduced both against `server.py`'s actual `_claude()` by stubbing the `mcp` import so the real module could be loaded and called directly. The commit-hook path is saved from user-visible damage only by an accident (`hooks/prepare-commit-msg` redirects `2>/dev/null` and only writes to the commit-message file on non-empty stdout, so the traceback is silently discarded and it falls back to git's own template) — the MCP tool path has no equivalent safety net; an uncaught exception there is just an uncaught exception. Distinct from the 2026-07-26 post: that one found two return statements disagreeing on formatting; this is a third code path that was never a return statement at all, live in the same six-line `try/except` block. Fixed: added `except subprocess.CalledProcessError` (with `stderr=subprocess.PIPE` on the call so the message can include the actual failure reason) and `except FileNotFoundError` to both `server.py`'s `_claude()` and `git_commit.py`, mirroring the existing `ERROR:`-prefixed / stderr-printed pattern each file already used for timeouts. Reran both repro cases against the fixed code — clean `ERROR: claude -p exited 1: simulated failure: rate limited` and `ERROR: claude CLI not found on PATH`, no tracebacks.
  2. Re-audited `scripts/check_key_facts.py` (built 2026-07-25 to catch `key_facts.md`'s Project Files table going stale) and found it only checks one direction — real files not mentioned in the docs — never the reverse. Checked whether the reverse direction had any live cases: `key_facts.md` documents `update_profile.py` ("Pushes `template.md` to GitHub profile README") and `template.md` ("Source of truth for GitHub profile README"), neither of which exists on disk. Confirmed this isn't a stale-after-deletion case (unlike `post_article.py`, which `key_facts.md` correctly documents as removed 2026-07-16): `git rev-parse --is-shallow-repository` is `false`, `git rev-list --all --count` is 44, and `git log --all --diff-filter=A --name-only -- update_profile.py template.md` returns nothing — neither file was ever committed to this repo, in any commit, ever. Yet `docs/project_notes/issues.md`'s 2026-06-21 entry claims "Created `template.md`... Auto-push via `update_profile.py`," and `decisions.md` has an ADR reasoning about `update_profile.py`'s design. Root cause of what actually happened outside this repo is unknowable from inside it; what's verifiable is narrower and still real — `key_facts.md`, the file this repo's own agents are told to trust instead of re-deriving "what scripts exist," asserted two working tools that a full, non-shallow history search proves were never part of the codebase. Distinct from the 2026-07-25 post that built this checker (that found real files with no docs — missing-from-docs) — this is the mirror bug (docs claiming a phantom file), worse in effect for an agent that trusts the file: a missing-from-docs script is invisible until needed; a phantom-in-docs script gets confidently referenced and only then found not to exist. Fixed: added `project_files_table_rows()` (isolates just the Project Files table's first column, not every backticked token in the file, since other rows/prose reference API paths and env var names that aren't filenames) and a second check in `main()` for table rows whose target doesn't exist on disk, with an explicit `.env` exception (documented as intentionally never committed, so its absence is by design). First run against the unfixed docs correctly flagged both phantom rows; removed them from `key_facts.md`; reran clean.
  - Tags: mcp, python, debugging, devtools; ai, python, devtools, debugging. Sources: `drafts/claude-p-wrapper-catches-timeout-not-nonzero-exit.md`, `drafts/key-facts-documents-phantom-files.md`. Code changes: `server.py` (`_claude()` now catches `CalledProcessError`/`FileNotFoundError` alongside `TimeoutExpired`, all three `ERROR:`-prefixed), `git_commit.py` (same two new `except` clauses), `scripts/check_key_facts.py` (new `project_files_table_rows()` + reverse phantom-file check), `docs/project_notes/key_facts.md` (removed `update_profile.py`/`template.md` rows — never committed to this repo).

Verified the diff/logging logic with an offline unit test against fake before/after states (deliberately did not test by mutating a real live article) — JSONL entry and diff both carried correct before/after pairs. `create_article` doesn't share this shape (a bad call there just makes an extra draft, cheap to notice) — `update_article` was the one tool where a plausible call and a wrong call took the identical code path.

### 2026-07-27 - DEV.to: 2 articles published (first run of the day)

- **Status**: Completed (both live; verified via HTTP 200 fetch of each URL)
- **Description**: Session started with HEAD detached from `main` again (same recurring provisioning symptom, `bugs.md` 2026-07-18), local `main` 7 commits behind `origin/main` — recovered with `scripts/sync-main.sh` before any git-writing work, fast-forwarded cleanly, no divergence. Quota check via live API showed 0 published today (new UTC day; last entry was 2026-07-26 second run), so this was the first article-publishing run of the day; published 2 per the normal 1-3 range. Ranked trending dev.to ai/llm/mcp/claudecode/agents/productivity posts by reactions + 3×comments; every top scorer was already excluded by prior runs' extensive logs ("The Dirty Secret Behind AI Agents (Demo)", "AI And Code Ownership", "Choose your Burden", "I gave my AI agent the ability to send email", "'Local' Solves Where Your Data Goes...", "I Connected 3 MCP Servers to One Agent" — no genuine multi-server angle, this repo runs one MCP server) or too close to already-mined veins ("My AI pentest agent reported 23 root shells..." — faked-tool-result vein; "Log Every Agent Invocation... Stop Hook" — too close to the 2026-07-20 "zero log lines" post; "Tool Schema Drift: The Silent Failure Mode..." — too close to the 2026-07-23 unpinned-contract post). Instead of forcing a trend match, re-audited this exact repo's own code for gaps neither trend-matched nor previously written up, and found two, both verified live and fixed this run:
  1. `reply_comments.py`'s `replied_by_me()` (used by `pending()` to decide whether a comment thread still needs a reply) walked a comment's entire reply tree and returned `True` the moment it found *any* message from me, anywhere in the subtree — not whether the *latest* message was mine. Verified live with a repro against the real function: a thread where I replied on 2026-07-25 and the commenter posted a fresh follow-up on 2026-07-26 still returned `replied_by_me() == True`, meaning `pending()` would silently and permanently skip that thread — the follow-up would never resurface in any future run's output. Distinct from both prior fixes to this same function's neighborhood: the 2026-07-24 "36 drafted, 0 posted" post (drafted-vs-posted, a human-pasting-step gap) and the 2026-07-26 "substring collision" fix (a wrong-identity-match false positive in the dedup check). This is neither an identity bug nor a posting-step bug — it's a recency bug: the check could not distinguish "I have replied to this thread" from "I have seen the latest message in this thread," and those diverge the instant the other side speaks again. Fixed: replaced `replied_by_me()` with `latest_message()` (walks the subtree by `created_at`, DEV.to's comment API includes an ISO-8601 timestamp on every node) + `needs_reply()` (true iff the newest message's author isn't me). Rewrote `--selftest` with three cases (answered-and-quiet, answered-then-followed-up, untouched) — all pass. Reran `pending()` against the live API afterward: no crash, no output change for today's actual comment state (today's real inbox doesn't happen to be sitting on this exact case, but the repro against synthetic data confirms the bug was live).
  2. Re-verified the `hooks/prepare-commit-msg` install fix from `bugs.md` 2026-07-26 (which added `scripts/install-hooks.sh` and confirmed it working in that session's clone) by checking `.git/hooks/` in *this* session's fresh container before doing anything else: `ls .git/hooks/` showed only git's stock `.sample` files — `prepare-commit-msg` was not installed, less than 24 hours after the prior run verified it was. Root cause: `.git/hooks/` is untracked and this repo's sessions are provisioned by checking out a pinned commit SHA into a fresh container each time (same mechanism as the recurring detached-HEAD bug, `bugs.md` 2026-07-18) — running `install-hooks.sh` once, in one container, has no effect on the next container's `.git/hooks/`, which starts empty again regardless of how many times the fix has already "worked." The 2026-07-26 fix's own prevention note ("run `scripts/install-hooks.sh` once per fresh clone/container") was already being silently skipped on the very next opportunity to test it, because nothing in the repo's actual workflow re-runs it — the note was advice to a future run that never reads bug logs before acting. Confirmed `install-hooks.sh` itself is correct and idempotent (reran it standalone, clean byte-for-byte copy, no error) — the gap is entirely that nothing calls it a second time. Distinct from the 2026-07-26 post itself (found the hook was never installed, anywhere, ever) and the 2026-07-22 "stale tracking ref" post (same pinned-SHA-checkout root cause, different artifact — a git ref, not a hooks directory) — this is about the *fix* for the first bug inheriting the same "doesn't survive a fresh container" property as the bug it fixed, caught only by literally checking the file the prior post's own prevention note pointed at. Fixed: moved the `install-hooks.sh` call into the top of `scripts/sync-main.sh`, which is already documented as safe to run unconditionally at the start of every git-writing session and already runs first, every run — so the hook install is now a side effect of something the workflow already does unconditionally, not a separate manual step. Verified: reran `sync-main.sh`, hook reappears and matches source byte-for-byte.
  - Tags: python/debugging/agents/devtools; git/python/debugging/devtools. Sources: `drafts/comment-pipeline-checks-ever-not-latest.md`, `drafts/hooks-install-fix-didnt-survive-next-container.md`. Code changes: `reply_comments.py` (`replied_by_me()` replaced with `latest_message()`/`needs_reply()`, `pending()` updated, `--selftest` rewritten), `scripts/sync-main.sh` (now installs hooks at the top of every run).

Ranked trending dev.to ai/llm/mcp/claudecode/agents/productivity posts by reactions + 3×comments; top scorers were either already excluded by prior runs' extensive logs ("The Dirty Secret Behind AI Agents (Demo)", "AI And Code Ownership", "Choose your Burden", "I built an AI dev harness that isn't allowed to trust itself" — all repeatedly flagged off-voice or too close to the self-distrust/verification vein already mined) or only tangentially relevant ("I Connected 3 MCP Servers to One Agent. It Got Scary Fast." — this repo only runs a single MCP server, no genuine multi-server angle to ground it in). Instead of forcing a trend match against a well this repo's own logs have repeatedly flagged as close to exhausted after 40+ posts, re-audited this exact repo's current code for gaps neither trend-matched nor previously written up, and found two, both verified live and fixed this run:
  1. `reply_comments.py`'s `pending()` used `if c["id_code"] in drafted` as its "already drafted" dedup check, where `drafted` is the *entire raw text* of `drafts/comment_replies.md` — a substring check against the whole file, not against actual header id_codes. Its neighbor function `audit()` (added 2026-07-25) does this correctly, extracting `set(re.findall(r"^## (\S+)", drafted_text, re.M))`. Verified live against the real draft file: a hypothetical new, never-drafted id_code `"2026"` (a plausible 4-char lowercase-alphanumeric shape, same as real id_codes like `3b908`) is NOT in the set of real headers, but IS found as a raw substring of the file's prose (an unrelated sentence mentioning the year). `pending()`'s old check would have silently skipped this comment forever — no error, no log line, nothing — while `audit()` (which only tracks id_codes actually extracted as headers) has no visibility into a comment that was never even detected as pending in the first place. Distinct from the 2026-07-24 "36 drafted, 0 posted" post (drafted-vs-posted) and the 2026-07-25 memory-files post (a docs table, not this pipeline's own dedup logic) — this is a live substring-collision risk in the pipeline's core dedup, caught by noticing `pending()` and `audit()` do the same lookup two different ways, forty lines apart. Fixed: `pending()` now extracts `drafted_codes` via the same anchored header regex `audit()` uses, checked with `in drafted_codes` instead of `in drafted`. Reran `--selftest` clean; reran the collision simulation against the fix — confirmed `"2026" in drafted_codes` is `False` where the old raw-text check was `True`.
  2. Re-audited `server.py`'s `_claude()` helper (used by the `generate_commit_message` MCP tool) after the 2026-07-24 empty-diff-guard fix, which introduced an `"ERROR: ..."` prefix convention so callers can do `result.startswith("ERROR:")` to detect a failure instead of trusting the string as a real commit message. That convention was never checked against `_claude()`'s other, older failure path: on `subprocess.TimeoutExpired` (added 2026-07-24 in a separate pass, fixing the "no timeout" bug from 2026-07-23/2026-07-24), it returned the bare string `"claude -p timed out after 20s"` — no `ERROR:` prefix. A caller that adopted the empty-diff fix's own advice (check the prefix) would get `False` on a timeout and commit the literal explanatory sentence as a Conventional Commit subject. Confirmed by direct code inspection (regex-extracted both return statements) rather than by triggering a real 20s hang. Distinct from both prior fixes that touched this exact function on the same days (2026-07-23 timeout-added, 2026-07-24 empty-diff-guard-added) — this is about the two fixes never being reconciled against each other's signaling convention. Fixed: changed the timeout branch to return `"ERROR: claude -p timed out after 20s"`, matching the empty-diff branch. Verified via `ast.parse` and a direct regex check that both `generate_commit_message` failure paths now share the same prefix.

  1. Re-verified the `hooks/prepare-commit-msg` timeout fix from `bugs.md` 2026-07-23 by checking whether the hook was actually reachable by git in this environment, not just correct on disk: `git config --get core.hooksPath` and `git config --global --get core.hooksPath` both returned empty, and `ls .git/hooks/` showed only git's stock `prepare-commit-msg.sample` — no real `prepare-commit-msg`. The hook has never executed once in this environment since the repo's first commit. Root cause: `hooks/` is a plain tracked directory with no special meaning to git; only `.git/hooks/` (or `core.hooksPath`) is ever executed, and neither is part of the tracked repo — README's own install instructions (`git config --global core.hooksPath d:/codes/my_git_manger/hooks`, a Windows-absolute path, or a manual `cp hooks/prepare-commit-msg .git/hooks/`) are both one-time, machine/clone-local steps that don't survive a fresh `git clone` or this repo's pinned-SHA container checkout. Fixed: added `scripts/install-hooks.sh` (copies `hooks/*` into `.git/hooks/`, chmod +x), ran it for real against this clone, verified installed content matches source byte-for-byte, and verified the hook's own `[ "$2" = "" ] || exit 0` guard behaves correctly both when a message source is given (no-op, file untouched) and when none is given (attempts to run `git_commit.py`, which correctly detected a clean working tree and left the file empty). Distinct from the 2026-07-23 post (fixed the hook's internal timeout, assumed it ran) and the 2026-07-24 comment-reply-pipeline post (a manual-paste gap, not a git-hooks-path wiring gap) — same "the fix on paper isn't the fix in practice" shape, different mechanism.
  2. Re-audited the `_STRIP` bare-substring filter flagged in the 2026-07-22 article ("kept signing itself" mirror bug: `"llm"`/`"claude"`/`"anthropic"` as bare substrings erase any commit message that legitimately mentions this project's own AI-calling code) and confirmed, by rerunning the exact same test cases, that it was still unfixed in both `server.py` and `git_commit.py` four days later — that audit had proposed narrower patterns but explicitly stopped short of shipping them. Shipped the fix this run: replaced the bare-substring tuple with a word-boundary/phrase-anchored regex (`co-authored-by\s*:`, `generated (with|by)\s+claude`, `\bclaude code\b`, `\bwritten by (an )?(ai|llm|claude|chatgpt|copilot)\b`, `\bai-generated\b`, `🤖`) in both files. Verified against two sets before shipping: legitimate technical lines (`"fix: retry llm calls on 429 with backoff"`, `"docs: explain the claude cli fallback path"`, etc.) now survive unchanged; real attribution lines (`"Co-Authored-By: Claude Sonnet 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>"`, `"🤖 Generated with Claude Code"`) are still stripped to empty. Confirmed both files compile cleanly post-edit. Distinct from the 2026-07-22 post itself (that was the audit-only finding) — this is the follow-through fix, and a note-to-self on treating "proposed a fix, didn't ship it" as a live TODO rather than a closed finding.

Ran a UA matrix live against `GET /api/articles/me`: confirmed the real rule is narrower and different in kind than documented — dev.to 403s any User-Agent containing the substring "urllib" (case-insensitive) or a missing/empty UA, not "anything that looks like a script." `python/1.0`, `foo-python-bar`, `curl/8.0`, and `PostmanRuntime/7.0` all pass clean; only strings containing "urllib" (including a made-up `foo-urllib-bar`) get blocked. This mattered concretely for `server.py`'s `_dev()` helper, which uses a third, undocumented UA string (`developer-presence-mcp/1.0`) that has silently worked since day one purely because it doesn't contain "urllib" — nobody chose it for that property, and no comment explained it. A well-intentioned rename to something more descriptive (e.g. containing "urllib-client") would have silently 403'd all four DEV.to MCP tools with an empty response body and no diagnosable error shape. Distinct from the 2026-07-22 "GitHub proxy-shaped 403" post (a sandbox-proxy artifact on a different platform/mechanism) — this is a real Forem/dev.to bot-detection substring rule on the live API, verified with a UA test matrix, not a proxy artifact. Fixed this run: corrected the misleading comments in both scripts to describe the tested substring rule, and added a documented `_DEV_UA` constant + assert guard in `server.py` so a future rename can't reintroduce the bug silently.
- **Quality-bar call**: Published 1 instead of forcing 2-3, per the task's own quality bar — today's second consecutive run where the trending sweep produced no second strong, genuinely distinct angle without drifting into already-mined veins. Repeating the flag from this morning's and 2026-07-23's entries: this repo's material is close to exhausted after 40+ posts against a small codebase; still worth a user decision on cadence, source-material scope, or a review gate.

Risk: a caller piping the tool's return value straight into a commit (the tool's entire purpose) would commit the literal explanatory sentence as the commit message, since nothing distinguishes it from a real one. Fixed this run: added `if not diff.strip(): return "ERROR: empty diff — nothing to generate a commit message from."` to `generate_commit_message` in `server.py`, mirroring the script's guard in a way a caller can check (`.startswith("ERROR:")`).

- **Explicit quality-bar call**: this is the ~32nd–33rd article from a very small 8-file repo (`server.py`, `git_commit.py`, `publish_devto.py`, `reply_comments.py`, `scripts/sync-main.sh`, `hooks/prepare-commit-msg`) across 10+ days of twice-daily runs. Nearly every file has already been mined multiple times (see `bugs.md`/`decisions.md` — most entries there already have a corresponding published article). Per the task's own quality bar ("publish fewer articles... rather than forcing weak ones"), published 1 instead of forcing 2–3.
- **What got published**: re-read `hooks/prepare-commit-msg` and `git_commit.py` closely (not reused from a prior write-up) and found a real, previously-undocumented bug: neither `subprocess.check_output` call in `git_commit.py` (the `git diff --staged` call or the `claude -p` call) had a `timeout`, so a hung `claude` CLI process would hang `git commit` indefinitely with no diagnostic. Separately, the hook's `python "$SCRIPT" > "$1" 2>/dev/null || true` truncates the commit-message file via shell redirect *before* Python produces any output, so any failure path (missing `claude` on PATH, a crash) wipes git's own pre-filled template to empty, silently, since stderr is discarded and `|| true` swallows the exit code — git's own empty-message-abort guardrail happened to prevent worse (empty-message commits), but that was luck, not design. Reproduced the hang deliberately (wrapper script that `sleep 300`s instead of the real `claude` binary; confirmed with `ps` that the hang was the subprocess, not git). Fixed both: added `timeout=20` + a `TimeoutExpired` handler to `git_commit.py`, and changed the hook to capture output into a shell variable first, only overwriting `$1` if the script exited zero with non-empty output — so every failure path now leaves git's default template alone instead of wiping it. Distinct from the 2026-07-19 "same code, agentic or not" post and the 2026-07-22 "commit message deletes any commit about LLMs" post — neither touched reliability/timeout/silent-truncation behavior.
- Tags: claudecode/python/debugging/devtools. Source: `drafts/commit-hook-llm-no-timeout.md`. Code changes: `git_commit.py`, `hooks/prepare-commit-msg`.
- https://dev.to/enjoy_kumawat/my-commit-hook-calls-an-llm-on-every-commit-it-had-no-timeout-so-neither-did-git-commit-bpj

Recent days' entries (2026-07-20 through 2026-07-22) show a visible drift toward increasingly self-referential meta-content (articles about the publishing log itself, about whether its own "distinct" claims are checked, about the pipeline's own quota logic). Also still open from this morning's first-run entry below: the unattended publish path (`publish_devto.py`, driven by this scheduled task) has no dry-run/hold-for-review step or kill switch, unlike the MCP tool's `published: bool = False` default — flagged, not fixed. Worth a human decision on cadence, source-material scope, or whether to add a review gate, rather than this routine continuing to self-diagnose it run after run.

Root-caused via `git reflog show refs/remotes/origin/main`: only two entries, the clone-time shallow fetch (which had set the tracking ref to a commit 14 behind the actual pinned detached-HEAD SHA) and the just-run push. Concluded the pinned-SHA checkout step that produces the detached HEAD fetches/checks out that specific commit object directly, which doesn't touch `refs/remotes/origin/main` the way a named-branch fetch would — leaving the tracking ref stale relative to HEAD from the moment the container boots, before any commands run. Fixed for real: added `git fetch origin main -q 2>/dev/null || true` to the top of `sync-main.sh` so any later "ahead of"/"behind" comparison is against fresh data. Distinct from the "wrong diagnosis" post (that covered why HEAD itself detaches) — this is about a different ref (the local tracking cache of the remote) going stale for a related but separate reason, previously only a one-line aside in the 2026-07-18 log, never root-caused or fixed until now.

Grounded in the actual helper code, a 4-line wrap-after-definition decorator that writes one structured JSON line per call, and an explicit warning against logging request headers (both helpers send bare credentials in an `Authorization`/`api-key` header — a naive "log everything on failure" version would leak them to a plaintext file). Distinct from the detached-HEAD and gitignore-drafts posts (both diagnosed a symptom from outside after the fact for code this session doesn't own) — this is about code the author owns end-to-end still requiring an external probe because it kept no record of its own.

Initially assumed the MCP version must have better safety handling (a stripped-attribution filter); verified by rereading `server.py` and found this was **wrong** — both paths apply the identical `_STRIP` filter, just structured differently: the script duplicates it inline, the tool inherits it for free via the shared `_claude()` helper every tool routes through. Corrected the draft before publishing rather than let the plausible-but-false claim stand. Distinct from the earlier FastMCP-decorators post (that was about the decorator library's schema-generation gaps) and the tool-schema-bloat post (upfront token cost) — this is about what "agentic" means at the interface-boundary level when the underlying call is provably identical code.

The 2026-07-15 article blamed concurrent agents sharing one working tree and prescribed `git worktree` isolation — but this is a single resumed session, not concurrent ones, and the theory never explained why the detached commits were already safely on `origin/main` (a push succeeding, not racing). Root cause is environment provisioning checking out a specific commit SHA rather than the `main` ref, independent of anything this repo's code does. Wrote `scripts/sync-main.sh` (idempotent, only ever fast-forwards, refuses to touch divergent history) and a corrected `bugs.md` entry. Distinct from the original worktree post — this one is about re-testing a published diagnosis against its own recurrence and finding the fix targeted the wrong layer, not about concurrent-session isolation.
  2. Real incident found while auditing this very task's Step 5 instruction ("commit drafts + the log"): `git log --all -- drafts/` is empty across 30+ published articles — `drafts/` has been gitignored since the repo's first commit, so every prior "committed drafts" claim silently only ever committed the log entry. Verified live (`git add drafts/<file>` exits 0 while printing an ignored-path hint). Turned the accidental gap into a deliberate one via `decisions.md` ADR-005 (drafts stay local/ephemeral; the log entry + live URL is the permanent record) rather than force-adding a growing pile of duplicate markdown into history. Distinct from the prior "silently swallowed errors" post (`2>/dev/null` hiding a real failure) — this is a command that isn't hiding anything; it's honestly reporting success for a smaller scope than assumed, and nobody had checked `git status` against the claim.

Real incident from `bugs.md` (2026-07-01, never turned into its own article) — the `vercel/eve` pre-commit hook's `ENOENT: git` error while committing the #412 fix, root-caused to `scripts/pre-commit-fmt.mjs` computing its `cwd` via `new URL("..", import.meta.url).pathname` instead of `fileURLToPath()`, which keeps a bogus leading slash before the Windows drive letter. Distinct from all prior posts — this is a general Node/Windows path-resolution debugging lesson (ENOENT from `spawnSync` can mean "bad cwd," not just "command not found"), not previously covered from this repo's incident log. Loosely paired with the "I Stopped Debugging at My Desk" trend (productivity, score 127) — the real lesson is debugging by causal chain (what fed the failing call a bad input) vs. debugging by symptom location (the command that happened to throw).
  2. Real, previously-undocumented-as-an-article bug found by rereading this repo's own `hooks/prepare-commit-msg`: the `python "$SCRIPT" > "$1" 2>/dev/null || true` line correctly keeps a broken `claude` CLI call from ever blocking a commit, but as written it also makes "the AI call crashed" and "the AI call ran and had nothing to say" render identically — an empty message buffer, no error, no trace, `2>/dev/null` throwing the reason away. Matched to the "My extension 'worked' — it just quietly saved half of every conversation" trend (productivity, score 46) — same shape of bug: a success signal that's actually silence hiding failure. Fix shown: keep the always-succeed guarantee but write the captured error into the message buffer as `#`-prefixed lines (git ignores them in the real commit, but they're visible in the editor) instead of swallowing it. Distinct from the prior "kept signing its own work" post (that was about a natural-language instruction not being self-enforcing for AI-attribution stripping) — this is about a shell hook's own failure-signaling design, unrelated to what the AI outputs when it succeeds.
  - Tags: debugging/javascript/git/devtools; ai/git/debugging/devtools. Sources: `drafts/enoent-git-was-never-the-problem.md`, `drafts/pre-commit-hook-silently-swallowed-failures.md`.
  - https://dev.to/enjoy_kumawat/my-git-commit-failed-with-enoent-git-git-was-never-the-problem-c1p
  - https://dev.to/enjoy_kumawat/my-ai-commit-hook-never-failed-a-single-commit-that-was-the-bug-2933

### 2026-07-14 - DEV.to run blocked: dev.to unreachable from sandbox egress policy
- **Status**: Blocked (no articles published, no quota consumed)
- **Description**: Scheduled DEV.to publishing run could not get past Step 1 (quota check). `dev.to:443` is not on the environment's egress allowlist — every outbound HTTPS call to it fails at the agent proxy with a hard `403` on `CONNECT` (`gateway answered 403 to CONNECT (policy denial or upstream failure)`, confirmed via `curl $HTTPS_PROXY/__agentproxy/status`). This is a hard organization policy denial, not a transient network error — per `/root/.ccr/README.md` these must not be retried or routed around (no CA/proxy workaround applies; the host itself is denied). No workaround exists from inside this sandbox; the environment's network policy needs `dev.to` added to the allowlist for this to run.
- **Action needed**: Either run the DEV.to publishing task from an environment whose egress policy allows `dev.to`, or get `dev.to` added to this environment's allowed egress hosts.

### 2026-07-01 - OSS Contribution to vercel/eve — issue #412

- **Status**: Completed (PR opened, awaiting review)
- **Description**: Forked + cloned `vercel/eve` (already set up: fork `enjoykumawat/eve`, local clone `D:\codes\eve`). Root-caused #412 ("subagent MODEL_CALL_FAILED gets swallowed") via systematic-debugging: `packages/eve/src/harness/tool-loop.ts`'s `classification === "terminal"` branch returned `{ done: true, output: "" }` unconditionally, never checking `config.mode`, unlike the non-terminal branch right below it which already special-cased `config.mode === "task"` to set `isError: true`. Result: a subagent whose model call failed terminally (401/403/404/invalid API key/unresolvable model id) rolled up into the parent orchestrator via `workflow-entry.ts`'s `finalizeDone` as a **successful** empty-output `subagent-result` — matches the issue's exact repro (404 from an unresolvable OpenRouter model slug). Fix: applied the same `config.mode === "task"` check to the terminal branch. Added a regression test in `tool-loop.test.ts` (confirmed red before fix, green after). Full `packages/eve` unit suite run before/after — no new failures (pre-existing Windows path-separator failures in unrelated CLI/nitro-host tests present on `main` too). Added a changeset.
- **URL**: https://github.com/vercel/eve/pull/454
- **Notes**: No GPG/SSH signing key configured in this environment — vercel/eve requires verified signatures on protected branches (CONTRIBUTING.md); committed with DCO sign-off (`-s`) only, flagged in the PR for re-signing before merge. Also hit and worked around (didn't fix) an unrelated pre-existing Windows bug in the repo's own `scripts/pre-commit-fmt.mjs`: computes `REPO_ROOT` via `new URL("..", import.meta.url).pathname`, which keeps a leading `/` before the drive letter on Windows (`/D:/codes/eve/`), producing a bogus `cwd` for `spawnSync` that fails with a misleading `ENOENT: git` error on every commit. Worked around via the script's own `SKIP_SIMPLE_GIT_HOOKS=1` escape hatch after manually running `oxfmt`/`oxlint` to replicate what the hook would have done.

### 2026-08-10 - DEV.to: 2 articles published (first run of the day)
- **Status**: Completed (both live; verified via HTTP 200 fetch of each URL)
- **Description**: Quota check via `scripts/list_all_published_titles.py` (paginated, 110 total historical) showed 0 published today, so this run published 2 per the normal 1-3 range. Scored trending dev.to ai/llm/mcp/claudecode/agents/productivity posts by reactions + 3×comments. Top scorers were mostly off-lane discuss/community threads or specific-product coverage (Kiro Crew, "How would you decide whether content is good or bad") — skipped per past precedent. Matched two mid-tier trends to genuinely new, live-verified incidents in this repo rather than forcing the top scorers:
  1. "claude -p: what headless Claude Code actually loads (and when --bare is the right call)" trend (claudecode tag, score 19) → distinct from the two prior CLAUDE.md posts (2026-07-16: referenced tools that don't exist; 2026-07-31: MCP server never attached) — those were about whether the *referenced tools* are real, this is about whether an unrelated headless subprocess call even *loads the file* at all. Ran a live probe: a bare `claude -p "..."` from this repo's root loads the full `CLAUDE.md` (4,404 bytes) into a one-shot commit-message completion in `git_commit.py` that has no use for it — confirmed via a diagnostic prompt returning `YES-SAW-CTX-RULES`. Investigated `--bare` as the fix per the trend's own framing; found it requires `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`/`apiKeyHelper` and explicitly refuses OAuth, which is exactly what this project's `key_facts.md` says `git_commit.py`/`server.py` rely on — `--bare` would have broken auth. Found and verified `--safe-mode` instead (drops CLAUDE.md/skills/plugins/hooks/MCP-server discovery, keeps OAuth) via the same probe (`NO-CTX-RULES`). Fixed for real: added `--safe-mode` to the `claude -p` invocations in both `git_commit.py` and `server.py`'s `_claude()` helper (same call shape, same fix, mirroring how twin-function fixes have been applied to both files before). Verified `git_commit.py --selftest` and `server.py` AST-parse clean after the edit.
  2. "I Counted the Assertions in Our Test Suite. I Wish I Hadn't." trend (agents tag, score 31) → reframed around `git_commit.py`'s own `--selftest` block: all 8 assertions test only `_STRIP_RE` (the attribution-stripping regex), none touch the file's actual 5 subprocess failure branches (git-diff timeout, empty diff, claude-p timeout, non-zero exit, missing `claude` binary on PATH) — the exact except-clauses this file has. Compared directly against `publish_devto.py`'s and `server.py`'s `--selftest` blocks, which do stub `urlopen`/`_gh`/`_dev` and drive real failure paths (missing-credential KeyError regression from `bugs.md` 2026-08-09). Root cause identified but not fixed this run: `git_commit.py` has no function boundary around its risky code (top-level script, unlike `publish_devto.py`'s `main()` or `server.py`'s `_gh()`/`_dev()`), so it can't be selftest-stubbed the same way without first being refactored into a callable — flagged as the concrete next step. Distinct from all prior "missing except clause" / "verified live not selftested" posts — this is about test-suite shape (which branches get assertions at all), not a specific missing except clause or an unfixed regression.
  - Tags: claudecode/ai/python/devtools; python/debugging/claudecode/ai. Sources: `drafts/headless-claude-p-loaded-my-whole-claude-md.md`, `drafts/my-commit-scripts-selftest-tests-the-wrong-half.md`.

- **Status**: Completed (both live; verified via HTTP 200 fetch of each URL)
- **Description**: Quota check via `scripts/list_all_published_titles.py` (paginated, 114 total historical) showed 0 published today, so this run published 2 per the normal 1-3 range (also satisfies the "if still 0 on the second run, publish at least 2" contingency in case this is that run). Matched two mid-tier trends to genuinely new, live-verified findings in this repo instead of forcing the top scorers:
  1. "I Gave My Agent One Signed Permission It Couldn't Mint Itself" trend (agents/security, score 37) → investigated this server's own least-privilege story instead of a hypothetical one. Found that `server.py`'s three GitHub tools (`get_github_profile`, `list_repos`, `get_repo_stats`) are all read-only, but `GITHUB_TOKEN` is scoped `repo, user` (full write access per `key_facts.md`) because other parts of the project need it — the MCP server just inherits the same `.env`. The only enforcement is `_gh()`'s `if method != "GET": raise ValueError` guard, added 2026-07-30 with a comment claiming it's "enforced, not just true by convention." Checked `--selftest` (which has grown a regression case for every other bug fixed in this file since late July) and found zero coverage of this specific guard — reproduced the gap live in isolation (stubbed the `mcp` import since this sandbox can't install it cleanly against system PyJWT; confirmed `_gh(..., method="POST")` raises with the guard present). Fixed for real this run: added two selftest assertions covering the non-GET-method and data-payload-on-GET paths; full `--selftest` reruns clean. Distinct from the prior 403/timeout GitHub-helper posts — those were about request failures, this is about an unexercised security guard on an intentionally over-scoped credential.
  2. "How Do You Build an Evaluation Harness for AI Agents?" trend (agents/testing, score 80) → turned the publishing pipeline's own trending-topic scoring formula (`reactions + 3*comments`) back on itself, something it's never been applied to in 114 runs. Pulled real stats for the most recent 30 published articles via `list_articles`'s underlying endpoint: median reactions 0, median comments 1, mean score 3.5, 3/30 articles scored a flat 0 despite each having a "distinct angle" writeup in this log. Top scorers (11 points) were both missing-`except`-clause posts; a zero-scorer was the credential-checks-flagged post, which by the topic-selection prompt's own criteria should have been a strong pick — no visible relationship between the novelty filter and reader response. Conclusion: the "distinct angle, not already covered" check is a novelty filter, not a quality filter, and this pipeline has been treating passing it as a proxy for the second thing. Flagged the concrete next step (per-category score tracking via `list_articles`, tags kept on published articles) as an open gap, not fixed this run — same honesty-over-forcing-it pattern as other unfixed-root-cause posts in this log.

### 2026-08-11 - DEV.to: 2 articles published (second run of the day)
- **Status**: Completed (both live; verified via HTTP 200 fetch of each URL)
- **Description**: Quota check via live API showed 2 published today already (from this morning's first run), well under the 5-article cap and not at 0, so the "second run, still 0 → at least 2" floor didn't apply — published 2 more per the normal 1-3 range, landing today's total at 4. Ranked trending dev.to ai/llm/mcp/claudecode/agents/productivity posts by reactions + 3×comments. Top scorers were either off-lane discuss/community threads ("I Recreated Management With AI," "The Review Tax") or matched veins already exhausted by this account's 116-post history (e.g. "When an AI agent says 'I ran the tests and they passed'" echoes the 2026-07-14 tests-pass post; "I Gave My Agent One Signed Permission It Couldn't Mint Itself" was this morning's own GitHub-token-scope post's source trend). The one candidate that looked genuinely fresh at a glance — "Claude Code /context vs /memory: check what loaded" — turned out on checking this log's own history to be a near-exact rehash of the 2026-07-16 post ("My Project's Own Instructions Told My Agent to Use Tools That Don't Exist," about this exact repo's `CLAUDE.md` context-mode rules referencing tools absent from this environment) — caught before drafting, not after. Given how thoroughly this small codebase has been mined (twin-function-drift, pagination, credential KeyError, retry/idempotency, missing-except, comment-tree-walking, attribution-regex, doc drift, self-referential checker gaps — all closed off), delegated a full fresh-angle audit to a subagent with the complete list of already-covered veins, instructed to read every file in `docs/project_notes/` in full plus all current source before proposing anything. It surfaced two genuinely new, independently-verified findings, both in `git_commit.py`'s `diff = subprocess.check_output(["git", "diff", "--staged"], text=True, ...)` / `claude -p` call chain — a call site with several prior fixes (2026-08-06 timeout, 2026-08-10 `--safe-mode`) that had never had its *input-content* or *input-size* assumptions audited, only its failure timing:
  1. `text=True` decodes git's diff output with the locale's default encoding (UTF-8); a staged file containing a non-UTF-8 byte (cp1252 smart quotes, a legacy CSV, any stray non-ASCII byte) raised an uncaught `UnicodeDecodeError` — the only existing `except` on this call was `TimeoutExpired`. Independently reproduced (not just trusted the subagent's report): staged a file with an appended `0xe9` byte in a scratch repo, ran the identical call, got the raw traceback. Worse, checked `hooks/prepare-commit-msg` directly: `2>/dev/null` drops the traceback and `&& [ -n "$MSG" ]` short-circuits on the non-zero exit, so the hook fails completely silently — indistinguishable from a hook that ran fine and had nothing to say. Fixed: added `except UnicodeDecodeError`, exiting through the script's own stderr+`SystemExit(1)` convention. Not added to `--selftest`: the diff-read runs at module top level before the `--selftest` early-exit, the same no-function-boundary gap already flagged as an open next step in this log's 2026-08-11 first-run entry — noted, not silently claimed as covered.
  2. `claude -p`'s prompt is a single argv element in both `git_commit.py` and `server.py`'s `_claude()`. `os.sysconf('SC_ARG_MAX')` on this machine is 2,097,152 bytes; independently reproduced the failure by matching the exact call shape (`subprocess.check_output(["true", "x" * 3_000_000])`) and confirming `OSError: [Errno 7] Argument list too long` — a class none of either function's `except` clauses (`TimeoutExpired`, `CalledProcessError`, `FileNotFoundError`) catches, since none is `OSError`. A large enough staged diff (lockfile regen, big refactor, vendored file) hits this. Fixed in both files: a 200,000-byte bound checked before the argv is built, failing through each file's own existing convention instead of crashing raw or silently truncating the diff and generating a summary from an incomplete one. Considered switching to stdin instead (no argv limit at all) but didn't ship it — couldn't confirm `claude -p`'s stdin-prompt support without an authenticated call, and shipping a verified bound beat guessing at unverified CLI behavior. Added a permanent `--selftest` regression case to `server.py` (oversized prompt → `ERROR:` string, no subprocess reached); `git_commit.py`'s check has the same top-level-script selftest gap as finding 1.
  - Both findings verified live and logged in `docs/project_notes/bugs.md` (2026-08-11, two entries) before drafting. Distinct from every prior fix to this exact call site (all previous rounds — timeout, `--safe-mode`, credential-KeyError, attribution-regex — concerned *when*/*how* the subprocess is invoked, never what it assumes about its own input's encoding or size) and from the twin-function-drift vein (these aren't two implementations diverging from each other — both files got the identical fix in this same run, no gap between them).
  - Tags: python/git/debugging/ai; python/ai/mcp/debugging. Sources: `drafts/commit-script-assumed-utf8-diff.md`, `drafts/claude-p-argv-too-long.md`.

### 2026-08-13 - DEV.to: 2 articles published (this run)
- **Status**: Completed (both live; verified via HTTP 200 fetch of each URL)
- **Description**: Quota check via `scripts/list_all_published_titles.py` (paginated, 122 total historical) showed 0 published today, so this run published 2 per the normal 1-3 range (also satisfies the "if still 0 on the second run, publish at least 2" contingency in case this is that run — this session had no way to distinguish first-run-of-day from second). Scored trending dev.to ai/llm/mcp/claudecode/agents/productivity posts by reactions + 3×comments. Top scorers were all either off-lane discuss/community essays ("I Recreated Management With AI," "You Don't Have an AI Problem You Have a Thinking Problem," "Stratagems #24") or a near-exact rehash of an already-covered vein ("When an AI agent says 'I ran the tests and they passed' — do you trust it?" echoes the 2026-07-14 tests-pass post). Given how exhaustively this repo's own code has already been mined (122 posts; CLAUDE.md/MANDATORY-framing, update_article's audit log, credential loading, idempotency/dedup, missing-except-clauses, comment-reply pipeline, twin-function drift, doc-drift/phantom-file, and attribution-regex veins are each 3-6+ articles deep — checked against the full `issues.md`/`bugs.md` log before drafting, not just this run's own memory), delegated a fresh-angle audit to a subagent instructed to read `issues.md` (827 lines) and `bugs.md` in full plus all current source, explicitly told which veins were exhausted, and asked for findings verifiably outside all of them. It surfaced two, both independently re-verified live in this run before drafting:
  1. `hooks/prepare-commit-msg`'s guard clause, `[ "$2" = "" ] || exit 0`, is commented "Skips merge commits, squash, and amend" — true, but it also skips `message` (git's `COMMIT_SOURCE` for `git commit -m`/`-F`), which the comment never mentions. Verified live in a scratch clone: `git commit -m "test"` sets `COMMIT_SOURCE=message` and exits before `git_commit.py` ever runs; only a bare interactive `git commit` (no `-m`) leaves `$2` empty and lets the hook's AI-generation path fire. This repo's own scheduled publishing routine — the thing that made all ten-plus prior fixes to this exact hook (timeout, argv-limit, UTF-8 decode, `--safe-mode`, repo-root resolution, six STRIP-regex passes) — commits exclusively via `git commit -m "$(cat <<'EOF' ... )"`, meaning the guard may have kept the hook from ever firing on a single one of this repo's real commits, including the commits that "fixed" it. Corroborated independently, not just via the code read: pulled the last 50 real commit subjects from `git log`, found 21 exceed the system prompt's stated "max 72 chars" (up to 98), several combining unrelated concerns a diff-only generator can't know about (e.g. citing a publish-run tally in a fix commit's subject) — consistent with these being typed by the orchestrating session via `-m`, not produced by the hook's own pipeline. Distinct from every prior fix to this hook (all assumed a firing hook and fixed *what happens inside it*; this is whether it fires at all for the one invocation shape this repo's automation actually uses) and from the "hook install path" vein (those fixed *where* the hook finds `git_commit.py`, assuming a correctly-installed hook already runs). Not fixed this run — which of several real fixes (loosen the guard, stop using `-m`, or accept the hook was never meant for headless automation) is a design call, flagged rather than guessed at.
  2. `server.py`'s `update_article` empty-payload guard (added 2026-08-08, closing the zero-fields case) checks whether a field was *given*, not whether it would *change* anything. Reproduced live against the real function with `_dev` stubbed: `update_article(article_id=123, title="Same Title It Already Has")`, where that title equals the article's current value, still fires a live GET and PUT, and `_log_article_update` — the audit log built specifically "so a bad write leaves a trace" — writes `{"fields_changed": ["title"], "title_before": "...", "title_after": "..."}` with before/after identical, indistinguishable in the log from a genuine edit. Distinct from every prior audit-log finding on this exact function (blind-overwrite, diff never covering `body_markdown`, log never persisted to git, cwd-relative log path) — all of those are the log being incomplete or absent; this is the log being complete, present, and a false positive. Not fixed this run: the fix (compare against `before` before deciding the payload is non-empty) requires reordering the fetch ahead of the guard, a behavior-shape decision (raise vs. silently drop unchanged fields vs. return a no-op flag) flagged rather than guessed at.
  - Tags: git, python, debugging, ai; mcp, python, debugging, ai. Sources: `drafts/commit-hook-guard-skips-message-source.md`, `drafts/update-article-guard-checks-presence-not-change.md`.
  - https://dev.to/enjoy_kumawat/my-commit-hook-has-ten-documented-fixes-its-own-guard-clause-may-have-kept-it-from-running-on-a-4d8e
  - https://dev.to/enjoy_kumawat/my-mcp-tools-empty-payload-guard-checks-whether-you-passed-a-field-it-never-checked-whether-the-1fi2

### 2026-08-13 - DEV.to: 2 articles published (second run of the day)
- **Status**: Completed (both live; verified via HTTP 200 fetch of each URL)
- **Description**: Quota check directly against `GET /api/articles/me/published` (not just the log) showed 2 already published today from the earlier run, so this run stayed inside the 1-3 range with the day's total capped at 5 (2+2=4, under cap; the "still 0 on second run" contingency didn't apply since today's total was already 2). Scored trending ai/llm/mcp/claudecode/agents/productivity posts by reactions + 3×comments; top scorers were again either off-lane discuss essays ("I Recreated Management With AI," "You Don't Have an AI Problem You Have a Thinking Problem") or adjacent-but-exhausted veins ("The Mechanical vs. The Semantic: What Happens When AI Memory is Wrong?" reads close to the FTS5/cross-session-memory vein this account already covered from a different code angle). Delegated a fresh-angle audit to a subagent with the exhausted-veins list (from this file + `bugs.md`, read in full) and instructions to verify findings live, not just by reading. It returned two, both independently re-verified in this run before drafting:
  1. `publish_devto.py`'s `parse()` frontmatter splitter, `text.lstrip().split("---", 2)`, only guards the too-few-delimiters case (the 2026-08-06 fix, closed and selftested). It never handles too many: a literal `---` substring inside a frontmatter *value* (an em-dash-style title like "My Before---After Refactor") gets consumed as the second split point before the real closing fence is reached. `len(parts)` still comes out to exactly 3 — the same count a correctly-closed block produces — so the existing guard clause never fires. Reproduced against the real `parse()` with that exact title: `meta` came back as `{'title': 'My Before'}`, `tags`/`published` never parsed at all, and the leftover frontmatter lines leaked into `body` as literal text. Because `published` never got extracted, `main()`'s `meta.get("published", "false")` default meant the article would silently post as an unpublished draft instead of erroring — which is exactly what happened when this run's own first draft, titled with a literal `` `---` `` describing the bug itself, got mis-parsed by the unpatched script and came back `DRAFTED` instead of `PUBLISHED`. Caught immediately via the live URL check this task requires, retitled to drop the literal delimiter, and the stray draft was updated in place (PUT `/api/articles/4388125`) to the corrected, published version rather than left as orphaned junk. Not fixed at the parser level this run — a line-based rewrite (split on lines that are exactly `---`, not on the substring anywhere in the text) is sketched in the article but not landed, pending a `--selftest` case.
  2. `scripts/check_key_facts.py` defines two different "is this file documented" checks that disagree: `project_files_table_rows()` correctly scopes to the `## Project Files` table's first column (its own docstring says so), but the actual missing-file check, `missing = [f for f in real_files() if f not in documented_files()]`, uses `documented_files()` instead — an unscoped regex over the *entire file*, where any backticked mention anywhere (a credentials-section aside, a "superseded by" note) counts as "documented" identically to a real table row. Reproduced against the live functions with a fixture where a filename appears only in prose, never in the table: `documented_files()` returned it as documented, `project_files_table_rows()` correctly excluded it, and the real `missing` list computation came back empty — a false "in sync" for a file with zero actual documentation. Confirmed today's real `key_facts.md` doesn't currently trigger this (every real filename happens to be backticked only inside its own row), so it's a live design gap, not yet a live incident. Distinct from the 2026-08-12 phantom-file fix in this same script, which closed the opposite direction (files asserted as real that aren't) in `decisions.md`'s checker, not this one. Not fixed this run: the one-line swap to `project_files_table_rows()` is sketched in the article but not landed, pending a `--selftest` case pinning the fixture.
  - Tags: python, debugging, devtools, ai; python, debugging, devtools, productivity. Sources: `drafts/frontmatter-second-dash-picks-wrong-fence.md`, `drafts/doc-drift-checker-missing-check-not-scoped-to-table.md`.
  - https://dev.to/enjoy_kumawat/my-frontmatter-parser-checks-for-too-few-delimiters-it-never-checked-for-too-many-2j45
  - https://dev.to/enjoy_kumawat/my-doc-drift-checker-has-two-different-ideas-of-documented-and-only-uses-the-wrong-one-1ao7

- **Status**: Completed (live; verified via HTTP 200 fetch of the URL). Second run of the day stopped at 1 article instead of the normal 1-3 range, same restraint the first run applied, for the same reason.
- **Description**: Quota check via live `GET /api/articles/me/published?per_page=30` showed 1 published today (this morning's run), so the "still 0 on second run → publish at least 2" contingency did not apply. Scored trending ai/llm/mcp/claudecode/agents/productivity posts by reactions + 3×comments before drafting anything. Two findings from that scan mattered more than the ranking itself:
  1. Independently corroborated the first run's flagged concern rather than taking it on faith: pulled the trending list fresh, and this account's *own* prior articles now appear inside it — "My MCP Tool's Empty-Payload Guard Checks Whether You Passed a Field...", "My Comment-Reply Pipeline Was Feeding Me Garbled HTML Entities...", "I Ran `claude -p` for One Commit Message...", "My Commit-Message Script Has 8 Assertions in --selftest..." all showed up as `mcp`/`claudecode`-tag trending posts in this run's own Step 2 query. The "find trending topics not yet covered" methodology is now partially reading back this account's own output as if it were fresh external signal — a feedback-loop problem in the pipeline itself, not just a title-style or subject-narrowness complaint.
  2. Tried two candidate angles before finding one that held up. "CLAUDE.md is tracked and gitignored in the same commit" (prompted by `Opus 5: The Cost of Instruction Conflicts`, score 42, trending) turned out to already be a published post (`drafts/claude-md-tracked-and-gitignored-same-commit.md`) — caught by checking before drafting, not after. A stale-facts audit of `key_facts.md` (prompted by "My memory auditor said half my agent's facts were dead. Three were.", agents tag, score 36) turned up nothing real on inspection: the "8 tools" line matches an actual count of 8 `@mcp.tool()`-equivalent functions in `server.py` (3 GitHub + 4 DEV.to + `generate_commit_message`), and the token-scope line was already corrected in a prior run. Rather than force either into an article, kept looking — and hit a genuinely new, first-hand incident live in this exact session: Step 1 of this run's own topic-dedup check (`Read` on this file, `issues.md`) failed outright with `File content (352.4KB) exceeds maximum allowed size (256KB)`. This file is 908 lines and growing by one entry per run with no rotation or size cap; `scripts/check_key_facts.py` explicitly excludes it from doc-drift checking as an "append-only historical log" (correct on correctness grounds) but nobody had ever separately asked whether "append-only" also means "eventually too large for the tool that's supposed to check it" (it does, as of today). Verified the failure firsthand (this run hit it, not a hypothetical), worked around it with `offset`/`limit` reads, and wrote up the gap plus a sketched (not landed) `scripts/search_issues.py` fix — same "propose, verify live, don't force a landed fix if it's not ready" pattern as most unfixed-root-cause posts in this log. Distinct from every prior doc-drift/phantom-file/stale-fact post: those were all about entries being *wrong*; this is about the file being *too large to check at all*, on the one file whose entire purpose is being checked before proposing new work.
  - Given (1) and the first run's own explicit request for sign-off before continuing "more of the same," did not use the remaining 1-2 articles' worth of headroom under the daily cap. Publishing this one, verified-live, genuinely distinct piece satisfies this run's stated minimum-1 floor; forcing a second or third piece today would mean either re-treading an exhausted vein or reaching for something off-lane just to hit a range, which the task's own quality bar explicitly says not to do. Notified the user directly (outside this log) with the concrete evidence above, since two consecutive runs hitting the same wall — one flagging it, one independently corroborating and extending it — is a call that shouldn't be made unilaterally a third time in a row without a response.
